> DEVELOPER

Implement the following plan:

# FLUME VAE: Red Flags → Green Lights

## Core Principles

### 1. Learn Before You Burn

**No destructive operations without extracting knowledge first.** Before removing, replacing, or deprecating any code, data, or artifact:

1. **Identify patterns** — What good ideas exist in the code being removed? What design decisions were sound even if the implementation failed?
2. **Identify anti-patterns** — What went wrong? What assumptions were incorrect? What traps should future work avoid?
3. **Store in vault** — Log all patterns via `vault_extract_pattern` and anti-patterns via `vault_log_decision` to the MCP vault. Knowledge compounds; deleted code doesn't.
4. **Only then proceed** with removal.

This applies to every phase of this spec, not just Sprint 0. When Phase 2 replaces Phase 1 components, the same discipline applies.

### 2. Test-Driven Development (Red → Green → Refactor)

**Every feature starts with a failing test.** No production code without seeing RED first.

| Step | Action | Verification |
|------|--------|-------------|
| **RED** | Write a test that describes the desired behavior. Run it. Watch it fail. | Test fails for the *right reason* (missing feature, not syntax error) |
| **GREEN** | Write the minimum code to make the test pass. Nothing more. | Test passes. All existing tests still pass. |
| **REFACTOR** | Improve code quality while keeping tests green. | All tests still pass after changes. |

**Applied to FLUME:**
- Each VAE component (encoder, decoder, loss, training loop) gets its test FIRST
- Evaluation metrics get tests that verify known inputs produce expected outputs BEFORE implementation
- Integration points (SemanticCache, JourneyTracker) get contract tests BEFORE wiring

**Recovery rule:** If production code was written first (hook fires), write the test immediately — don't revert working code, but apply TDD properly going forward.

### 3. Compound Engineering

**Every feature makes future features easier.** The compound loop applies to this spec:

```
Implement feature → Validate manually → Write 5 tests → Extract learning → Refine skill
```

| Principle | Application in This Spec |
|-----------|-------------------------|
| **Knowledge compounds** | Every anti-pattern logged to vault prevents re-occurrence. Every pattern extracted is reusable. |
| **Build on proven templates** | New EmbeddingProvider follows existing `ExperienceCollector` multi-tier fallback pattern. New VAE loss follows existing `FlumeVAETrainer` structure (improved, not reinvented). |
| **Experience-guided execution** | Query vault for prior learnings before each sprint (lesson-37 validates this). |
| **Skill refinement loop** | After Sprint 3 evaluation, log results as vault experiment. Learnings flow back into Sprint 4 decisions. |
| **Honest metrics** | Report actual numbers (98.8% beats inflated 100%). Green-light criteria are pass/fail, not "close enough." |

### 4. Context Awareness

**Every session starts informed.** No cold starts.

| Checkpoint | Action |
|------------|--------|
| **Sprint start** | Query vault: `vault_find_relevant_context(query="FLUME VAE training")`. Read continuation file if exists. Check existing experiment notes. |
| **Before building** | Query Context7 for library docs (PyTorch VAE patterns, nomic-embed-text API). Don't assume — verify. |
| **Mid-sprint** | Check `cz context --json`. If >80%, prepare handoff. Write continuation with EXACT state. |
| **Sprint end** | Log to vault: decisions made, metrics achieved, patterns discovered. Next session starts informed. |

**Overnight simulation data** (5.5M trajectories) is a context asset. Log its characteristics to vault so Phase 2 sessions can find it without re-discovering.

### 5. Token Efficiency

**Budget tokens like money.** Based on CLAUDE.md token budgets:

| Task Type | Budget | Pattern |
|-----------|--------|---------|
| Implement 1 feature | 500-1,500 tokens | Copy template → code → manual test → 5 tests |
| Research + implement | 2,000-3,000 tokens | Quick survey → proof of concept → tests |
| Single test debug | 200-500 tokens | Add print → run → check → fix → verify |

**Anti-patterns to avoid:**
- Research-first (5,000-10,000 tokens for no implementation)
- Infrastructure for products that don't exist (8,000+ tokens)
- Running full test suite repeatedly (use `-k` to target specific tests)

**Efficiency tactics for this spec:**
- Use `uv run pytest tests/flume/ -k "test_vae"` not `uv run pytest tests/ -q` during development
- Cache Ollama embeddings in Sprint 1 LRU cache — avoid redundant API calls during training
- Batch embed texts (50 at a time) instead of one-by-one
- Mock Ollama in tests — never call live Ollama in unit tests
- Write narrow tests (one behavior per test) — faster to run, faster to debug

### 6. Evidence Over Assumptions

Every red flag became a red flag because someone assumed something worked without checking. Every claim in this spec has a concrete verification command. If you can't run a command to prove it, you can't claim it.

### 7. Phased with Hard Gates

No Phase 2 work begins until ALL Phase 1 green-light criteria are met with evidence. "Probably fine" is not a gate-pass.

---

## Context

The FLUME investigation (2026-02-23) identified 6 red flags — critical unknowns that determine whether FLUME is a viable research artifact or an architectural dead end. Codebase exploration revealed the situation is worse than the investigation assumed: **the system the investigation analyzed doesn't actually exist.** Two incompatible VAE implementations, a broken data pipeline training on random noise, and zero integration with agent behavior mean the red flags aren't "unknowns to measure" — they're **architectural problems to solve.**

This plan starts by freeing system resources (7 GB RAM from zombie processes, 7.4 GB disk from stale artifacts, 2,000+ lines of dead code), then rebuilds FLUME from first principles in two phases, turning each red flag green with concrete, verifiable criteria. It also maps FLUME's capabilities to the Anthropic Universes team Research Engineer role to validate strategic alignment.

```bash
free -h                                # Confirm RAM recovered
python -c "from cohezion.flume import FlumeVAEEncoder"  # Core import works
```

### Knowledge Extraction (BEFORE any deletions)

**Rule: No destructive operations without learning.** Before removing any code, extract patterns and anti-patterns into the MCP vault. This preserves institutional knowledge for future sessions.

**Anti-patterns to document (via `vault_log_decision` / `vault_extract_pattern`):**

| Anti-Pattern | Source File | Lesson |
|-------------|------------|--------|
| Dual VAE architecture | `autoencoder.py` + `training.py` | Two models operating on different input spaces creates integration debt that compounds silently. One coherent model > two partial ones. |
| Training on random noise | `dataset.py` (SyntheticFlumeDataset) | VAE trained on N(0.5, 0.15) learns Gaussian structure, not semantic structure. Synthetic data must have the *kind* of structure you want the model to learn. |
| SHA-256 as semantic embedding | `vae_encoder.py` + `experience_encoder.py` | Cryptographic hashes destroy semantic similarity. "deploy API" and "API deployment" hash to completely unrelated 256D vectors. Never use hash functions where semantic distance matters. |
| Hash-based journey tracking | `journey_tracker.py` | Deterministic text hashing for 12D trajectories means semantically identical tasks map to different points. Observable AI requires *meaningful* representations, not arbitrary ones. |
| Disconnected modules | morphospace, bioelectric, lcsp, etc. | 1,353 lines of code with zero consumers = pure maintenance burden. If a module has no imports, it's not "future work" — it's dead weight. Enforce "no orphan modules" policy. |
| HIHO coherence loss on batch mean | `training.py` line 118-120 | Penalizing `mean(μ) → 0.5` at batch level doesn't ensure per-sample coherence. Regularization must target the right granularity. |
| Character-level tokenizer | `tokenizer.py` + `autoencoder.py` | 74-char vocabulary for semantic embeddings produces poor representations. Always use subword tokenization (or better: pre-trained embeddings). |
| Zombie test processes | System observation | Pytest processes with async tests can hang indefinitely if event loops aren't properly torn down. Always set test timeouts (`pytest-timeout`). |

**Patterns worth preserving (good ideas in dead code):**

| Pattern | Source File | Worth Keeping |
|---------|------------|---------------|
| Morphospace stability wells | `morphospace.py` | Concept of "stability wells" around HIHO=0.5 in latent space is sound theory. Re-implement in Phase 2 with real learned latent space. |
| Trajectory prediction with physics | `predictor.py` | Momentum-based prediction and counterfactual branching (`imagine_branches()`) are good Phase 2 features. Preserve the API design. |
| Multi-tier data collection | `experience_collector.py` | Parquet → SurrealDB → Vault → synthetic fallback hierarchy is good architecture. Reuse in new data pipeline. |
| ExperienceEncoder layout | `experience_encoder.py` | The `[12D trajectory | 12 metrics | 5 op_type | ...]` prefix structure is well-designed. Keep for Phase 2, replace only the 227D fingerprint segment. |
| Checkpoint format with config | `training.py` | Saving `{model, optimizer, config, epoch, metrics}` together is correct practice. Carry forward to new training pipeline. |
| LCSP concept | `lcsp.py` | Latent Coherence Stability Predictor concept is valid for Phase 2 — predicting whether a latent point is in a stable region. |
| Bioelectric field modeling | `bioelectric.py` | Mapping latent gradients to "field strength" for action generation is creative. Document for Phase 2 investigation. |

**Vault storage plan:**
1. Log each anti-pattern as a `vault_log_decision` (decision: "don't do X because Y")
2. Extract each preservable pattern as a `vault_extract_pattern` (with code examples from the dead modules)
3. Log the overall cleanup as a `vault_log_experiment` (hypothesis: "dead code removal improves system health")

## Architecture: Fresh FLUME VAE

### Core Insight

Don't learn semantic embeddings from scratch with a tiny model. Use a **pre-trained embedding model** (Ollama `nomic-embed-text`, 768D) as input, then train a **proper VAE** to learn a structured, compressed 256D latent space on top of those embeddings.

### Phase 1 Architecture: Semantic Cache Engine

```
Text Input
    │
    ▼
[Ollama nomic-embed-text]  ─── 768D dense semantic embedding
    │
    ▼
[FLUME VAE Encoder]         ─── MLP: 768→512→512→384 → mu(256), logvar(256)
    │
    ▼
256D latent z (mu used as embedding at inference)
    │
    ▼
[FLUME VAE Decoder]         ─── MLP: 256→384→512→512→768 (reconstruct input)
    │
    ▼
[SemanticCache]             ─── cosine similarity matching (threshold ~0.88)
```

**Why this works:**
- Pre-trained embeddings already capture "deploy API" ≈ "API deployment"
- VAE learns *structured* 256D space where distances are meaningful
- KL regularization ensures smooth latent space (no holes between clusters)
- Decoder forces VAE to retain information (reconstruction validates quality)
- ~2.1M parameters — trains on CPU in minutes

### Loss Function

```
L = L_recon + β·L_KL + λ_align·L_contrastive + λ_coh·L_coherence

L_recon       = MSE(decoded, original_embedding)
L_KL          = -0.5·Σ(1 + logvar - μ² - exp(logvar))    with free-bits per dimension
L_contrastive = InfoNCE over known paraphrase pairs        (τ=0.07)
L_coherence   = (mean(μ) - 0.5)²                          HIHO target
```

**KL collapse prevention (Red Flag 1):**
- β annealing: 0.0 → 0.1 over first 30% of training steps
- Free-bits: min 0.125 bits/dimension — forces each dimension to encode information
- Active unit monitoring: alert if <128 of 256 dimensions are active

### Phase 2 Architecture: Reasoning Latent Space

Extends Phase 1 with a **temporal encoder** over trajectory sequences:

```
Trajectory Steps [(12D_state, metrics, op_type), ...]
    │
    ▼
[Step Embedding]       ─── Linear(29→128) + positional encoding
    │
    ▼
[Transformer Encoder]  ─── 2 layers, 4 heads, d_model=128
    │
    ▼
[Attention Pooling → mu(256), logvar(256)]
    │
    ▼
[Transformer Decoder]  ─── autoregressive trajectory reconstruction
```

Enables: interpolation between strategies, novel trajectory sampling, latent space navigation for agent guidance. JourneyTracker transitions from SHA-256 hashing to learned FLUME projections.

## Training Data Strategy (Hybrid)

### Bootstrap (Phase 1 — immediate)

1. **Structured synthetic tasks** (~5,000 samples)
   - Task descriptions across 5 operation types (generate, analyze, search, transform, persist)
   - Template groups with known paraphrases: `["deploy API", "API deployment", "launch API service"]`
   - Each embedded via `nomic-embed-text` → 768D vectors

2. **Codebase docstrings** (~2,000 samples)
   - Extract from Cohezion source via AST parsing
   - Real domain-specific language

3. **Vault experiment descriptions** (variable, ~500+ samples)
   - From `~/vaults/cohezion-vault/experiments/*.json`

4. **Paraphrase generation** (~15,000 pairs)
   - Use Ollama to generate 3 paraphrases per task template
   - Provides contrastive training pairs

### Fine-tune (accumulates over time)

5. **Real execution data pipeline**
   - After each compound execution: store `(task_description, operation_type, metrics, skill_name)` as JSONL
   - Path: `data/flume/experiences/execution_log.jsonl`
   - Periodic re-embed + re-train (weekly or on-demand)

### External (Phase 2)

6. **Published reasoning datasets** (GSM8K chains, MATH solutions, code generation traces)
   - For trajectory-level training in Phase 2

## Universes Team Alignment (Red Flag 6)

**Source:** [Research Engineer, Universes](https://job-boards.greenhouse.io/anthropic/jobs/5061517008)

### Mapping: Team Needs → FLUME Capabilities

| Universes Team Need | FLUME Capability | Alignment |
|---------------------|------------------|-----------|
| **Agentic training environments** for extended-duration tasks | Compound engineering loop: 11-step execution pipeline with skill refinement, retrospection, and journey tracking. Literally an RL training environment for agentic tasks. | **Strong** |
| **Evaluation frameworks measuring genuine capability gains** | Red Flag 4 resolution produces a validated evaluation framework with correlation-tested metrics. Phase 1 evaluation suite (reconstruction fidelity, similarity preservation, KL health, cache ablation) demonstrates evaluation engineering rigor. | **Strong** |
| **Navigate ambiguity, manage interruptions, sustain context** | Phase 2 reasoning latent space compresses reasoning trajectories for context sustainability. 256D embeddings preserve semantic meaning across long interactions. Morphospace navigation handles ambiguity via stability wells. | **Strong** (Phase 2) |
| **RL environments and simulation platforms** | 12D universe simulation with FLUME VAE as the representation layer. JourneyTracker records state transitions. DegradationDetector monitors drift. This is RL infrastructure. | **Strong** |
| **Integrate into production training systems** | Phase 1 semantic cache engine is a production system. Demonstrates ability to ship ML infrastructure, not just research prototypes. | **Strong** |
| **Rapidly troubleshoot across research and production ML** | This spec itself — diagnosing architectural fractures, designing fixes, implementing with TDD — demonstrates this capability directly. | **Meta** |

### Preferred Qualifications Match

| Qualification | Evidence |
|---------------|----------|
| LLM training/fine-tuning/evaluation | VAE training pipeline, evaluation framework, metric validation |
| RL environments, simulation platforms | 12D universe simulation, compound loop as RL env |
| Large-scale ML infrastructure | SemanticCache, ExperienceCollector, multi-tier data pipeline |
| Containerization, distributed systems | Cloud Run deployment, Tailscale Funnel for MCP, multi-session worktree pattern |

### Positioning Document (Deliverable)

Produce a concise document (max 2 pages) that:
1. Frames FLUME as an **agentic training environment** with validated evaluation
2. Highlights the **evaluation engineering** (not just the model)
3. Shows **production readiness** (Phase 1 cache engine in production)
4. Positions the **compound engineering loop** as an RL training methodology
5. Demonstrates research taste: honest red-flag identification, systematic resolution

### Key Integration Points

**`vae_encoder.py`** (primary integration surface):
```
Current: text → SHA-256 hash → expand to 256D → MLP encoder → mu → 256D
New:     text → Ollama embed → 768D → VAE encoder → mu → 256D
         (fallback: text → SHA-256 hash → expand to 256D if Ollama unavailable)
```

**`semantic_cache.py`** (consumer — no interface changes):
```
FlumeVAEEmbeddingModel.encode(text) → EmbeddingResult(embedding=[...256 floats...])
# Same interface, better embeddings
```

**`journey_tracker.py`** (Phase 1 minimal change):
```
text_to_latent(text):
    if flume_encoder available:
        flume_256d = flume_encoder.encode(text)
        return np.tile(flume_256d, 8)  # 256×8 = 2048D for existing pipeline
    else:
        return hash_based_latent(text)  # existing behavior
```

## Verification Strategy

### Per Red Flag

| Red Flag | Verification Command/Method | Pass Criteria |
|----------|----------------------------|---------------|
| 1. KL collapse | `uv run python -m scripts.train_flume_v2 --evaluate-kl` | Active units ≥ 128/256, KL > 0.1 |
| 2. Reconstruction | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k reconstruction` | cos_sim(input, recon) > 0.95 on validation |
| 3. Synthetic quality | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k paraphrase` | Precision@1 > 0.85 |
| 4. Metrics validation | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k similarity_preservation` | Spearman ρ > 0.90 between original and latent similarities |
| 5. Integration impact | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k cache_ablation` | FLUME precision-recall dominates hash baseline |
| 6. Team fit | Read positioning document, verify 3+ alignment points | Qualitative: convincing narrative |

### Full Suite

```bash
# Unit tests
uv run pytest tests/flume/ -v

# Train and evaluate
uv run python scripts/train_flume_v2.py --config default --evaluate

# Integration test
uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -v

# Existing test suite (no regressions)
uv run pytest tests/ -q
```

## Adversarial Review Findings

Three-perspective review per vault protocol (ADR 2026-02-14). All findings addressed in the plan above.

### Feasibility Challenger

| Finding | Severity | Resolution |
|---------|----------|------------|
| `nomic-embed-text` not installed in Ollama | HIGH | Added Task 0b: `ollama pull nomic-embed-text` |
| 768D→256D is only 3x compression — bottleneck may not force meaningful structure learning | MEDIUM | Contrastive loss (InfoNCE) provides additional learning signal beyond reconstruction. If bottleneck proves insufficient, reduce decoder capacity or add dropout. Monitor active units. |
| ~22,500 training samples for 2.1M params = overfitting risk | MEDIUM | Weight decay (1e-4), dropout (0.1), early stopping (patience=10), and data augmentation (paraphrases). Phase 1 is a starting point; real data accumulates. |
| Ollama network dependency at inference | MEDIUM | Fallback chain in EmbeddingProvider: Ollama → cached embeddings → SHA-256 hash. Cache hot embeddings in LRU cache (text→768D). |
| Paraphrase generation quality depends on Ollama model | LOW | Use simple templates + programmatic augmentation (word reordering, synonym substitution) before LLM generation. LLM paraphrases are supplementary, not primary. |

### Strategic Reviewer

| Finding | Severity | Resolution |
|---------|----------|------------|
| Phase 1 (cache engine) doesn't demonstrate RL capability — weak positioning | HIGH | Move positioning document to Sprint 1 (not Sprint 4). Frame Phase 1 as "evaluation engineering rigor" and Phase 2 as "RL environment." The investigation and systematic red-flag resolution ARE the demonstration of research engineering quality. |
| Overnight simulation data (5.5M trajectories) = irreplaceable Phase 2 training data | CRITICAL | Changed from "delete" to "compress and preserve." Added data characterization task. |
| Green-light criteria are internally defined — may not match what Universes team values | MEDIUM | Positioning document (moved to Sprint 1) explicitly maps our metrics to team needs. Adjust criteria if mapping reveals misalignment. |
| Experience-guided execution (lesson-37) validates hybrid data approach | LOW | Confirmed — our plan aligns with proven vault lesson. |

### Risk & Completeness Reviewer

| Finding | Severity | Resolution |
|---------|----------|------------|
| experience_collector/encoder/pipeline/dataset must be preserved | HIGH | No files deleted — all existing code stays on disk. New code supersedes, doesn't replace. |
| Vault's 6-step process (ADR 2026-02-09) — EXTRACT step is critical | HIGH | Sprint 0 extracts anti-patterns and patterns to vault before building new code |
| Overnight simulation data is irreplaceable Phase 2 training data | CRITICAL | Preserved. Stats logged to vault in Sprint 0. |
| Existing FLUME checkpoints should be examined | LOW | Remain on disk for reference. Can compare old vs. new model quality. |
| lesson-30 (holographic projection fallback) directly relevant to JourneyTracker integration | LOW | Already addressed — Phase 1 JourneyTracker change uses FLUME→tile→existing pipeline, avoiding projection issues. Phase 2 uses learned Linear(256→12) which doesn't need eigendecomposition. |

### Vault Insights Incorporated

| Vault Entry | Insight | Applied Where |
|-------------|---------|---------------|
| ADR: No Destructive Operations (2026-02-09) | Full 6-step process is mandatory | Sprint 0 restructured to follow all 6 steps |
| ADR: Adversarial Review Protocol (2026-02-14) | 3-agent review catches integration bugs | This review section + plan verification |
| Lesson 30: Holographic Projection Fallback | Singular matrix guard needed for dimensionality reduction | JourneyTracker integration design |
| Lesson 37: Experience-Guided Execution | Prior context materially improves quality | Validates hybrid data strategy |
| Experiment: First Real VAE Training (2026-02-13) | Started but empty — previous attempt didn't complete | Our plan fills this experiment with real results |
| Existing lessons 1-38 | 38 accumulated lessons available for pattern reuse | Referenced throughout plan design |

---

### Go/No-Go Gate (Phase 1 → Phase 2)

Phase 2 proceeds only if ALL Phase 1 green-light criteria are met:
- [ ] KL healthy (128+ active units)
- [ ] Reconstruction fidelity (cos_sim > 0.95)
- [ ] Paraphrase discrimination (P@1 > 0.85)
- [ ] Similarity preservation (ρ > 0.90)
- [ ] Cache A/B positive (FLUME beats hash)
- [ ] Real execution data collection pipeline operational

     1→"""FLUME VAE Encoder for production semantic embeddings.
     2→
     3→Wraps the trained FLUME VAE encoder to generate real semantic embeddings
     4→for cache similarity matching. Replaces deterministic hash-based embeddings
     5→with learned 256D latent representations.
     6→
     7→Features:
     8→- Load pre-trained VAE encoder from checkpoint
     9→- Generate 256D semantic embeddings from text
    10→- Support fallback to hash-based embeddings if VAE unavailable
    11→- Deterministic encoding for reproducibility
    12→- GPU support with CPU fallback
    13→"""
    14→
    15→import hashlib
    16→import logging
    17→from pathlib import Path
    18→
    19→import numpy as np
    20→
    21→
    22→try:
    23→    import torch
    24→    import torch.nn as nn
    25→
    26→    TORCH_AVAILABLE = True
    27→except ImportError:
    28→    TORCH_AVAILABLE = False
    29→
    30→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    31→
    32→
    33→class SimpleEncoder(nn.Module):
    34→    """Encoder to match VAE checkpoint structure.
    35→
    36→    Matches the sequential encoder: Linear(256->512) + ReLU + Linear(512->512)
    37→    """
    38→
    39→    def __init__(self, input_size: int = 256, hidden_size: int = 512):
    40→        """Initialize encoder.
    41→
    42→        Args:
    43→            input_size: Input embedding dimension (256)
    44→            hidden_size: Hidden layer dimension (512)
    45→        """
    46→        super().__init__()
    47→        self.encoder = nn.Sequential(
    48→            nn.Linear(input_size, hidden_size),
    49→            nn.ReLU(),
    50→            nn.Linear(hidden_size, hidden_size),
    51→        )
    52→
    53→    def forward(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
    54→        """Encode input through layers."""
    55→        return self.encoder(x)
    56→
    57→
    58→class FlumeVAEEncoder:
    59→    """Production VAE encoder for semantic embeddings."""
    60→
    61→    DEFAULT_MODEL_PATH = Path("./data/flume/checkpoints/flume_vae_ep50.pt")
    62→    EMBEDDING_DIM = 256
    63→
    64→    def __init__(
    65→        self,
    66→        model_path: Path | None = None,
    67→        device: str = "cpu",
    68→        fallback_to_hash: bool = True,
    69→    ):
    70→        """Initialize VAE encoder.
    71→
    72→        Args:
    73→            model_path: Path to VAE checkpoint (default: ep50.pt)
    74→            device: Device to load model on ("cpu" or "cuda")
    75→            fallback_to_hash: If True, use hash embeddings if VAE load fails
    76→        """
    77→        self.device = device
    78→        self.model_path = model_path or self.DEFAULT_MODEL_PATH
    79→        self.fallback_to_hash = fallback_to_hash
    80→        self.encoder = None
    81→        self.mu_head = None
    82→        self.enabled = False
    83→
    84→        if TORCH_AVAILABLE:
    85→            self._load_encoder()
    86→        elif not fallback_to_hash:
    87→            logger.warning("PyTorch not available and fallback disabled")
    88→
    89→    def _load_encoder(self) -> None:
    90→        """Load encoder from checkpoint."""
    91→        try:
    92→            if not self.model_path.exists():
    93→                logger.warning(f"Model path not found: {self.model_path}")
    94→                return
    95→
    96→            # Load checkpoint — weights_only=True prevents arbitrary code execution
    97→            checkpoint = torch.load(self.model_path, map_location=self.device, weights_only=True)
    98→
    99→            # Extract encoder and mu_head
   100→            encoder_state = checkpoint.get("encoder")
   101→            mu_state = checkpoint.get("mu_head")
   102→
   103→            if encoder_state is None or mu_state is None:
   104→                logger.warning("Encoder or mu_head not found in checkpoint")
   105→                return
   106→
   107→            # Create and load encoder
   108→            # Encoder outputs 512D (hidden_size), not 256D
   109→            self.encoder = SimpleEncoder(input_size=256, hidden_size=512)
   110→            # The checkpoint stores the sequential module directly, not under "encoder"
   111→            self.encoder.encoder.load_state_dict(encoder_state)
   112→            self.encoder.to(self.device)
   113→            self.encoder.eval()
   114→
   115→            # Create and load mu_head (512 -> 256)
   116→            self.mu_head = nn.Linear(512, self.EMBEDDING_DIM)
   117→            self.mu_head.load_state_dict(mu_state)
   118→            self.mu_head.to(self.device)
   119→            self.mu_head.eval()
   120→
   121→            self.enabled = True
   122→            logger.info(f"Loaded FLUME VAE encoder from {self.model_path}")
   123→
   124→        except Exception as e:
   125→            logger.warning(f"Failed to load VAE encoder: {e}")
   126→            self.encoder = None
   127→            self.mu_head = None
   128→
   129→    def encode(self, text: str) -> np.ndarray:
   130→        """Encode text to 256D semantic embedding.
   131→
   132→        Uses VAE encoder if available, falls back to hash embedding otherwise.
   133→
   134→        Args:
   135→            text: Text to encode
   136→
   137→        Returns:
   138→            256D numpy array, normalized to unit length
   139→        """
   140→        if self.enabled and self.encoder is not None:
   141→            return self._vae_encode(text)
   142→        elif self.fallback_to_hash:
   143→            return self._hash_encode(text)
   144→        else:
   145→            raise RuntimeError("VAE encoder not available and fallback disabled")
   146→
   147→    def _vae_encode(self, text: str) -> np.ndarray:
   148→        """Encode using trained VAE encoder.
   149→
   150→        Args:
   151→            text: Text to encode
   152→
   153→        Returns:
   154→            256D normalized embedding
   155→        """
   156→        try:
   157→            with torch.no_grad():
   158→                # Generate initial embedding from text hash
   159→                hash_embedding = self._hash_encode(text)
   160→                hash_tensor = torch.from_numpy(hash_embedding).float().unsqueeze(0).to(self.device)
   161→
   162→                # Pass through encoder and mu_head
   163→                encoded = self.encoder(hash_tensor)
   164→                latent = self.mu_head(encoded)
   165→
   166→                # Convert to numpy and normalize
   167→                embedding = latent.squeeze(0).cpu().numpy().astype(np.float32)
   168→                norm = np.linalg.norm(embedding)
   169→                if norm > 0:
   170→                    embedding /= norm
   171→
   172→                return embedding
   173→
   174→        except Exception as e:
   175→            logger.debug(f"VAE encoding failed: {e}, using hash fallback")
   176→            return self._hash_encode(text)
   177→
   178→    @staticmethod
   179→    def _hash_encode(text: str) -> np.ndarray:
   180→        """Encode using deterministic hash (fallback).
   181→
   182→        Args:
   183→            text: Text to encode
   184→
   185→        Returns:
   186→            256D normalized embedding from SHA-256 hash
   187→        """
   188→        hash_obj = hashlib.sha256(text.encode())
   189→        hash_bytes = hash_obj.digest()
   190→
   191→        embedding = np.zeros(256, dtype=np.float32)
   192→        for i in range(256):
   193→            byte_idx = i % len(hash_bytes)
   194→            embedding[i] = hash_bytes[byte_idx] / 255.0
   195→
   196→        # Normalize
   197→        norm = np.linalg.norm(embedding)
   198→        if norm > 0:
   199→            embedding /= norm
   200→
   201→        return embedding
   202→
   203→    def is_available(self) -> bool:
   204→        """Check if VAE encoder is available."""
   205→        return self.enabled
   206→
   207→
   208→# Global encoder instance
   209→_encoder_instance: FlumeVAEEncoder | None = None
   210→
   211→
   212→def get_encoder() -> FlumeVAEEncoder:
   213→    """Get or create singleton VAE encoder."""
   214→    global _encoder_instance
   215→    if _encoder_instance is None:
   216→        _encoder_instance = FlumeVAEEncoder()
   217→    return _encoder_instance
   218→
   219→
   220→def reset_encoder() -> None:
   221→    """Reset encoder instance (for testing)."""
   222→    global _encoder_instance
   223→    _encoder_instance = None
   224→
```

```json
{
  "project": "cohezion",
  "title": "Never use SHA-256 hashes as semantic embeddings",
  "context": "FLUME vae_encoder.py uses SHA-256 hash bytes expanded to 256D as input to the VAE encoder. This means 'deploy API' and 'API deployment' hash to completely unrelated 256D vectors despite being semantically identical.",
  "decision": "Replace SHA-256 hash embeddings with pre-trained semantic embeddings (Ollama nomic-embed-text, 768D) as VAE input. Keep hash as last-resort fallback only.",
  "rationale": "Cryptographic hashes are designed to maximize distance between similar inputs. Semantic embeddings are designed to minimize distance between similar meanings. Using hashes where semantics matter is an anti-pattern that makes the entire downstream pipeline (VAE, cache, journey tracking) semantically meaningless.",
  "alternatives_considered": "1. Train character-level tokenizer (existing autoencoder.py approach) — too small vocabulary (74 chars), poor representations. 2. Use sentence-transformers — requires separate pip install, nomic-embed-text already available via Ollama."
}
```

{
  "project": "cohezion",
  "title": "Never train VAE on random noise as synthetic data",
  "context": "FLUME dataset.py SyntheticFlumeDataset generates training data from N(0.5, 0.15) — pure random noise. The VAE learns Gaussian structure, not semantic structure. This means the model's latent space reflects noise distribution, not meaningful task relationships.",
  "decision": "Synthetic training data must have the KIND of structure you want the model to learn. For semantic VAE: use pre-trained embeddings of real text with known paraphrase relationships for contrastive learning.",
  "rationale": "A VAE is a universal function approximator for distributions. If you train it on N(0.5, 0.15), it learns to reconstruct Gaussians perfectly — which is useless for semantic tasks. The training data distribution defines what the model learns."
}
```

{
  "project": "cohezion",
  "title": "One coherent model beats two partial implementations",
  "context": "FLUME had two VAE implementations: autoencoder.py (character-level tokenizer, 74-char vocab, encoder-decoder) and training.py (256D input from experience_encoder, separate encoder/decoder/mu/logvar heads). They operated on completely different input spaces and were never unified.",
  "decision": "Build one VAE (vae.py) that takes 768D pre-trained embeddings as input and produces 256D latent space. Single model, single input format, single training pipeline.",
  "rationale": "Dual implementations create integration debt that compounds silently. Neither model can be validated because each covers only part of the pipeline. Testing, debugging, and reasoning about system behavior becomes impossible when two models exist for the same conceptual purpose."
}
```

```json
{
  "project": "cohezion",
  "title": "HIHO coherence loss must target per-sample not batch mean",
  "context": "FLUME training.py (line 118-120) penalizes batch mean of mu toward 0.5 (HIHO target). This allows individual samples to have arbitrary mu values as long as they average to 0.5 across the batch.",
  "decision": "Apply HIHO coherence loss as (mean(mu_per_sample) - 0.5)^2 on individual sample means, not batch-level mean. Each sample's latent representation should center around HIHO target.",
  "rationale": "Batch-level regularization doesn't ensure per-sample coherence. A batch where half the samples are at 0.0 and half at 1.0 has a batch mean of 0.5 but no individual sample achieves HIHO stability."
}
```

{
  "project": "cohezion",
  "title": "Enforce no-orphan-modules policy",
  "context": "FLUME had 1,353+ lines across disconnected modules (morphospace.py, bioelectric.py, lcsp.py, mnm.py, vliw_kernel_sim.py, vliw_latent_alignment.py, alignment.py, navigator.py, benchmark_swarm_s16.py) with zero consumers in the codebase. These modules had valid concepts but no integration.",
  "decision": "Modules with no imports from production code are dead weight. Preserve concepts in vault patterns, but don't maintain orphan code. New modules must have at least one consumer at creation time.",
  "rationale": "Orphan modules aren't 'future work' — they're maintenance burden. If nobody imports a module, nobody tests it, nobody updates it when APIs change, and nobody knows if it works. The concepts are valuable; the code without consumers is not."
}

{
  "project": "cohezion",
  "title": "Always set pytest timeouts for async tests",
  "context": "Found 12 zombie pytest processes running 25+ hours, consuming 7 GB RAM. All were running async test suites that hung indefinitely when event loops weren't properly torn down.",
  "decision": "Use pytest-timeout plugin with default timeout. Set PYTEST_TIMEOUT=120 (2 min) globally or use @pytest.mark.timeout(60) per test. Never let async tests run unbounded.",
  "rationale": "Async tests with improper event loop cleanup can hang forever. Without timeouts, these become zombie processes that accumulate RAM. 12 zombies × ~600 MB each = 7 GB wasted."
}
```

{
  "project": "cohezion",
  "title": "Character-level tokenizers are inadequate for semantic embeddings",
  "context": "FLUME autoencoder.py used a 74-character vocabulary tokenizer for semantic embedding. This produces poor representations because single characters carry almost no semantic information. 'deploy' becomes [d,e,p,l,o,y] — six tokens with no meaning individually.",
  "decision": "Use pre-trained subword/sentence embeddings (nomic-embed-text via Ollama, 768D) instead of training character-level representations from scratch. The embedding model has been trained on billions of tokens and already understands semantic similarity.",
  "rationale": "Pre-trained models encode vast linguistic knowledge. A 74-char vocab trained on small domain data cannot compete. Use pre-trained embeddings as input to a VAE that learns structured compression, not raw text understanding."
}

{

  "title": "Hash-based journey tracking produces meaningless 12D trajectories",
  "context": "JourneyTracker uses deterministic text hashing for text_to_latent(), meaning semantically identical tasks ('deploy API' vs 'API deployment') map to completely different 12D trajectory points. Observable AI requires meaningful representations.",
  "decision": "Phase 1: Add optional FLUME encoder path in text_to_latent() that produces semantically meaningful vectors. Phase 2: Replace hash path entirely with learned Linear(256→12) projection from FLUME latent space.",
  "rationale": "The purpose of journey tracking is to observe semantic relationships between agent actions. Hash-based tracking destroys these relationships by design. FLUME embeddings preserve semantic similarity, enabling meaningful trajectory analysis."
}

The morphospace defines attractive regions in latent space where agents naturally converge. Valid theory for Phase 2 implementation with a real learned latent space where distances are meaningful.",

  "description": "Trajectory prediction using momentum (velocity + acceleration) in latent space, plus counterfactual branching via imagine_branches() that explores alternative trajectories. Good Phase 2 feature: given a current trajectory in FLUME latent space, predict next states and explore 'what-if' branches.",
  "code_example": "```python\n# From predictor.py - API design worth preserving\nclass LatentPredictor:\n    def predict_next(self, trajectory: list[np.ndarray], steps: int = 1) -> list[np.ndarray]:\n        \"\"\"Predict next latent states using momentum.\"\"\"\n        velocity = trajectory[-1] - trajectory[-2]\n        acceleration = velocity - (trajectory[-2] - trajectory[-3]) if len(trajectory) > 2 else 0\n        return [trajectory[-1] + velocity * (i+1) + 0.5 * acceleration * (i+1)**2 for i in range(steps)]\n    \n    def imagine_branches(self, trajectory: list[np.ndarray], n_branches: int = 3) -> list[list[np.ndarray]]:\n        \"\"\"Generate counterfactual trajectory branches by perturbing momentum.\"\"\"\n        # Each branch varies the velocity direction slightly\n```",

}

{
  "source_path": "src/cohezion/flume/experience_collector.py",
  "pattern_name": "Multi-Tier Data Collection with Graceful Fallback",
  "description": "Three-tier data collection: Tier 1 (Parquet shards, fastest), Tier 2 (SurrealDB queries, medium), Tier 3 (Vault JSON files, slowest). Each tier is non-blocking with try/except fallback. If any tier fails, remaining tiers still contribute. Reuse this pattern in the new FLUME data pipeline for embedding collection.",
  "code_example": "```python\n# From experience_collector.py - multi-tier fallback pattern\nclass ExperienceCollector:\n    def collect_all(self, max_samples=100_000):\n        records = []\n        # Tier 1: Parquet (fastest)\n        try:\n            records.extend(self._collect_parquet(max_samples))\n        except Exception as e:\n            logger.warning(f\"Parquet collection failed: {e}\")\n        # Tier 2: SurrealDB (medium)\n        try:\n            remaining = max_samples - len(records)\n            if remaining > 0:\n                records.extend(self._collect_surreal(remaining))\n        except Exception as e:\n            logger.warning(f\"SurrealDB collection failed: {e}\")\n        # Tier 3: Vault JSON (slowest, always available)\n        try:\n            remaining = max_samples - len(records)\n            if remaining > 0:\n                records.extend(self._collect_vault(remaining))\n        except Exception as e:\n            logger.warning(f\"Vault collection failed: {e}\")\n        return records\n```",
  "domain": "general"
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/src/cohezion/flume/embedding_provider.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Embedding providers for FLUME VAE input.\n\nThree providers with fallback chain:\n  1. OllamaEmbeddingProvider — 768D via nomic-embed-text (best quality)\n  2. HashFallbackProvider — 256D via SHA-256 (always available, no semantics)\n  3. CachedEmbeddingProvider — LRU cache wrapper around any provider\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport hashlib\nimport logging\nfrom collections import OrderedDict\nfrom typing import Protocol\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport requests\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n\nclass EmbeddingProvider(Protocol):\n    \"\"\"Protocol for embedding providers.\"\"\"\n\n    @property\n    def embedding_dim(self) -> int: ...\n\n    def embed(self, text: str) -> np.ndarray: ...\n\n    def embed_batch(self, texts: list[str]) -> np.ndarray: ...\n\n\nclass OllamaEmbeddingProvider:\n    \"\"\"Embed text via Ollama nomic-embed-text (768D).\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        model: str = \"nomic-embed-text\",\n        base_url: str = \"http://localhost:11434\",\n        timeout: float = 30.0,\n    ) -> None:\n        self.model = model\n        self.base_url = base_url.rstrip(\"/\")\n        self.timeout = timeout\n\n    @property\n    def embedding_dim(self) -> int:\n        return 768\n\n    def embed(self, text: str) -> np.ndarray:\n        \"\"\"Embed single text to 768D normalized vector.\"\"\"\n        try:\n            resp = requests.post(\n                f\"{self.base_url}/api/embed\",\n                json={\"model\": self.model, \"input\": text},\n                timeout=self.timeout,\n            )\n            resp.raise_for_status()\n            data = resp.json()\n            vec = np.array(data[\"embeddings\"][0], dtype=np.float32)\n            norm = np.linalg.norm(vec)\n            if norm > 0:\n                vec /= norm\n            return vec\n        except Exception as e:\n            raise ConnectionError(f\"Ollama embedding failed: {e}\") from e\n\n    def embed_batch(self, texts: list[str]) -> np.ndarray:\n        \"\"\"Embed batch of texts. Returns (N, 768) array.\"\"\"\n        try:\n            resp = requests.post(\n                f\"{self.base_url}/api/embed\",\n                json={\"model\": self.model, \"input\": texts},\n                timeout=self.timeout,\n            )\n            resp.raise_for_status()\n            data = resp.json()\n            vecs = np.array(data[\"embeddings\"], dtype=np.float32)\n            norms = np.linalg.norm(vecs, axis=1, keepdims=True)\n            norms = np.where(norms > 0, norms, 1.0)\n            return vecs / norms\n        except Exception as e:\n            raise ConnectionError(f\"Ollama batch embedding failed: {e}\") from e\n\n\nclass HashFallbackProvider:\n    \"\"\"Deterministic SHA-256 hash embedding (256D). No semantic meaning.\"\"\"\n\n    @property\n    def embedding_dim(self) -> int:\n        return 256\n\n    def embed(self, text: str) -> np.ndarray:\n        \"\"\"Embed text to 256D via SHA-256 hash expansion.\"\"\"\n        hash_bytes = hashlib.sha256(text.encode()).digest()\n        vec = np.zeros(256, dtype=np.float32)\n        for i in range(256):\n            vec[i] = hash_bytes[i % len(hash_bytes)] / 255.0\n        norm = np.linalg.norm(vec)\n        if norm > 0:\n            vec /= norm\n        return vec\n\n    def embed_batch(self, texts: list[str]) -> np.ndarray:\n        \"\"\"Embed batch of texts. Returns (N, 256) array.\"\"\"\n        return np.stack([self.embed(t) for t in texts])\n\n\nclass CachedEmbeddingProvider:\n    \"\"\"LRU cache wrapper around any EmbeddingProvider.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, inner: EmbeddingProvider, max_size: int = 10_000) -> None:\n        self._inner = inner\n        self._max_size = max_size\n        self._cache: OrderedDict[str, np.ndarray] = OrderedDict()\n\n    @property\n    def embedding_dim(self) -> int:\n        return self._inner.embedding_dim\n\n    def embed(self, text: str) -> np.ndarray:\n        if text in self._cache:\n            self._cache.move_to_end(text)\n            return self._cache[text]\n        result = self._inner.embed(text)\n        self._cache[text] = result\n        if len(self._cache) > self._max_size:\n            self._cache.popitem(last=False)\n        return result\n\n    def embed_batch(self, texts: list[str]) -> np.ndarray:\n        return np.stack([self.embed(t) for t in texts])\n\n\ndef create_embedding_provider(\n    *,\n    use_cache: bool = True,\n    cache_size: int = 10_000,\n    require_ollama: bool = False,\n) -> EmbeddingProvider:\n    \"\"\"Create the best available embedding provider.\n\n    Tries Ollama first, falls back to hash if unavailable.\n    \"\"\"\n    provider: EmbeddingProvider\n    if require_ollama:\n        provider = OllamaEmbeddingProvider()\n    else:\n        try:\n            ollama = OllamaEmbeddingProvider()\n            # Quick probe to check availability\n            ollama.embed(\"test\")\n            provider = ollama\n            logger.info(\"Using Ollama nomic-embed-text (768D)\")\n        except Exception:\n            provider = HashFallbackProvider()\n            logger.info(\"Ollama unavailable, using hash fallback (256D)\")\n\n    if use_cache:\n        provider = CachedEmbeddingProvider(provider, max_size=cache_size)\n\n    return provider\n"
}

loss_dict[\"recon_loss\"].item() > 0\n        assert loss_dict[\"total_loss\"].item() > 0\n\n    def test_kl_loss_is_positive(self):\n        \"\"\"KL divergence should be positive for non-standard def test_kl_loss_zero_for_standard_normal(self):\n        \"\"\"KL should be near zero when mu=0, logvar=0 (standard normal).\"\"\"\n        from cohezion.flume.vae import flume_vae_loss\n\n        x = torch.randn(8, 768)\n        mu = torch.zeros(8, 256)\n        logvar = torch.zeros(8, 256)\n\n        loss_dict = flume_vae_loss(x, x.clone(), mu, logvar, beta=1.0, free_bits=0.0)\n\n        assert loss_dict[\"kl_loss\"].item() < 0.01\n\n    def test_free_bits_clamps_kl(self):\n        \"\"\"Free-bits should enforce minimum KL per dimension.\"\"\"\n        from cohezion.flume.vae import flume_vae_loss\n\n        x = torch.randn(8, 768)\n        mu = torch.zeros(8, 256)\n        logvar = torch.zeros(8, 256)\n\n        # With free_bits=0, KL ~0 for standard normal\n        loss_no_fb = flume_vae_loss(x, x, mu, logvar, beta=1.0, free_bits=0.0)\n        # With free_bits=0.125, KL should be >= 0.125 * 256\n        loss_fb = flume_vae_loss(x, x, mu, logvar, beta=1.0, free_bits=0.125)\n\n        assert loss_fb[\"kl_loss\"].item() >= loss_no_fb[\"kl_loss\"].item()\n\n    def test_beta_scales_kl_contribution(self):\n        \"\"\"Higher beta should increase KL contribution to total loss.\"\"\"\n        from cohezion.flume.vae import flume_vae_loss\n\n        x = torch.randn(8, 768)\n        recon = torch.randn(8, 768)\n        mu = torch.ones(8, 256)\n        logvar = torch.zeros(8, 256)\n\n        loss_low = flume_vae_loss(x, recon, mu, logvar, beta=0.01)\n        loss_high = flume_vae_loss(x, recon, mu, logvar, beta=1.0)\n\n        assert loss_high[\"total_loss\"].item() > loss_low[\"total_loss\"].item()\n\n    def test_coherence_loss_penalizes_deviation_from_hiho(self):\n        \"\"\"Coherence loss should be higher when mean(mu) deviates from 0.5.\"\"\"\n        from cohezion.flume.vae import flume_vae_loss\n\n        x = torch.randn(8, 768)\n        mu_centered = torch.full((8, 256), 0.5)\n        mu_off = torch.full((8, 256), 2.0)\n        logvar = torch.zeros(8, 256)\n\n        loss_centered = flume_vae_loss(x, x, mu_centered, logvar, lambda_coherence=1.0)\n        loss_off = flume_vae_loss(x, x, mu_off, logvar, lambda_coherence=1.0)\n\n        assert loss_off[\"coherence_loss\"].item() > loss_centered[\"coherence_loss\"].item()\n\n\nclass TestModelConfig:\n    \"\"\"Test model parameter counts and configuration.\"\"\"\n\n    def test_parameter_count_reasonable(self):\n        \"\"\"Model should have ~2M parameters (not too large for CPU training).\"\"\"\n        from cohezion.flume.vae import FlumeVAE\n\n        model = FlumeVAE(input_dim=768, latent_dim=256)\n        total_params = sum(p.numel() for p in model.parameters())\n\n        # Should be between 1M and 4M params\n        assert 1_000_000 < total_params < 4_000_000, f\"Got {total_params} params\"\n\n    def test_different_latent_dims(self):\n        \"\"\"Model should work with different latent dimensions.\"\"\"\n        from cohezion.flume.vae import FlumeVAE\n\n        for latent_dim in [64, 128, 256]:\n            model = FlumeVAE(input_dim=768, latent_dim=latent_dim)\n            x = torch.randn(2, 768)\n            recon, mu, logvar, z = model(x)\n            assert mu.shape == (2, latent_dim)\n            assert recon.shape == (2, 768)\n"

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/src/cohezion/flume/vae.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"FLUME VAE v2 — Semantic latent space from pre-trained embeddings.\n\nArchitecture:\n  Encoder: 768 → 512 → 512 → 384 → mu(256), logvar(256)\n  Decoder: 256 → 384 → 512 → 512 → 768\n\nLoss: L_recon + β·L_KL (with free-bits) + λ_coh·L_coherence\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport torch\nimport torch.nn as nn\nimport torch.nn.functional as F\n\n\nclass FlumeVAE(nn.Module):\n    \"\"\"Variational autoencoder for semantic embedding compression.\n\n    Takes pre-trained 768D embeddings (e.g., nomic-embed-text) and learns a\n    structured 256D latent space where distances are semantically meaningful.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        input_dim: int = 768,\n        latent_dim: int = 256,\n        dropout: float = 0.1,\n    ) -> None:\n        super().__init__()\n        self.input_dim = input_dim\n        self.latent_dim = latent_dim\n\n        # Encoder: input_dim → 512 → 512 → 384\n        self.encoder = nn.Sequential(\n            nn.Linear(input_dim, 512),\n            nn.LayerNorm(512),\n            nn.GELU(),\n            nn.Dropout(dropout),\n            nn.Linear(512, 512),\n            nn.LayerNorm(512),\n            nn.GELU(),\n            nn.Dropout(dropout),\n            nn.Linear(512, 384),\n            nn.LayerNorm(384),\n            nn.GELU(),\n        )\n\n        # Latent heads\n        self.mu_head = nn.Linear(384, latent_dim)\n        self.logvar_head = nn.Linear(384, latent_dim)\n\n        # Decoder: latent_dim → 384 → 512 → 512 → input_dim\n        self.decoder = nn.Sequential(\n            nn.Linear(latent_dim, 384),\n            nn.LayerNorm(384),\n            nn.GELU(),\n            nn.Dropout(dropout),\n            nn.Linear(384, 512),\n            nn.LayerNorm(512),\n            nn.GELU(),\n            nn.Dropout(dropout),\n            nn.Linear(512, 512),\n            nn.LayerNorm(512),\n            nn.GELU(),\n            nn.Linear(512, input_dim),\n        )\n\n    def encode(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:\n        \"\"\"Encode input to (mu, logvar).\"\"\"\n        h = self.encoder(x)\n        return self.mu_head(h), self.logvar_head(h)\n\n    def decode(self, z: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:\n        \"\"\"Decode latent vector to reconstruction.\"\"\"\n        return self.decoder(z)\n\n    def reparameterize(\n        self, mu: torch.Tensor, logvar: torch.Tensor\n    ) -> torch.Tensor:\n        \"\"\"Sample z using reparameterization trick. In eval mode, return mu.\"\"\"\n        if self.training:\n            std = torch.exp(0.5 * logvar)\n            eps = torch.randn_like(std)\n            return mu + eps * std\n        return mu\n\n    def forward(\n        self, x: torch.Tensor\n    ) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:\n        \"\"\"Full forward pass: encode → reparameterize → decode.\n\n        Returns (reconstruction, mu, logvar, z).\n        \"\"\"\n        mu, logvar = self.encode(x)\n        z = self.reparameterize(mu, logvar)\n        recon = self.decode(z)\n        return recon, mu, logvar, z\n\n\ndef flume_vae_loss(\n    x: torch.Tensor,\n    recon: torch.Tensor,\n    mu: torch.Tensor,\n    logvar: torch.Tensor,\n    *,\n    beta: float = 0.1,\n    free_bits: float = 0.125,\n    lambda_coherence: float = 0.01,\n) -> dict[str, torch.Tensor]:\n    \"\"\"Compute FLUME VAE loss with KL annealing support and free-bits.\n\n    Returns dict with individual loss components and total.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Reconstruction: MSE\n    recon_loss = F.mse_loss(recon, x, reduction=\"mean\")\n\n    # KL divergence per dimension with free-bits\n    kl_per_dim = -0.5 * (1 + logvar - mu.pow(2) - logvar.exp())\n    # Free-bits: clamp minimum KL per dimension\n    if free_bits > 0:\n        kl_per_dim = torch.clamp(kl_per_dim, min=free_bits)\n    kl_loss = kl_per_dim.mean()\n\n    # HIHO coherence: per-sample mean(mu) should be near 0.5\n    sample_means = mu.mean(dim=1)  # (batch,)\n    coherence_loss = ((sample_means - 0.5) ** 2).mean()\n\n    total = recon_loss + beta * kl_loss + lambda_coherence * coherence_loss\n\n    return {\n        \"total_loss\": total,\n        \"recon_loss\": recon_loss,\n        \"kl_loss\": kl_loss,\n        \"coherence_loss\": coherence_loss,\n    }\n"
}

== 100\n\n    def test_covers_all_operation_types(self):\n        \"\"\"Generated tasks should span all 5 operation types.\"\"\"\n        from cohezion.flume.data_pipeline import SyntheticTaskGenerator\n\n        gen = SyntheticTaskGenerator()\n        tasks = gen.generate(n=500)\n\n        op_types = {t[\"op_type\"] for t in tasks}\n        assert op_types == {\"generate\", \"analyze\", \"search\", \"transform\", \"persist\"}\n\n    def test_each_task_has_required_fields(self):\n        \"\"\"Each task should have text, op_type, and group_id.\"\"\"\n        from cohezion.flume.data_pipeline import SyntheticTaskGenerator\n\n        gen = SyntheticTaskGenerator()\n        tasks = gen.generate(n=10)\n\n        for t in tasks:\n            assert \"text\" in t\n            assert \"op_type\" in t\n            assert \"group_id\" in t\n            assert isinstance(t[\"text\"], str)\n            assert len(t[\"text\"]) > 0\n\n\nclass TestContrastivePairMiner:\n    \"\"\"Test contrastive pair mining from task groups.\"\"\"\n\n    def test_same_group_pairs(self):\n        \"\"\"Should find pairs from same group (positive pairs).\"\"\"\n        from cohezion.flume.data_pipeline import ContrastivePairMiner\n\n        tasks = [\n            {\"text\": \"deploy API\", \"group_id\": \"g1\"},\n            {\"text\": \"API deployment\", \"group_id\": \"g1\"},\n            {\"text\": \"run tests\", \"group_id\": \"g2\"},\n            {\"text\": \"execute tests\", \"group_id\": \"g2\"},\n        ]\n        miner = ContrastivePairMiner()\n        pairs = miner.mine_pairs(tasks)\n\n        # Should have at least 2 positive pairs\n        assert len(pairs) >= 2\n        for anchor_idx, positive_idx in pairs:\n            assert tasks[anchor_idx][\"group_id\"] == tasks[positive_idx][\"group_id\"]\n\n    def test_returns_index_pairs(self):\n        \"\"\"Pairs should be tuples of (anchor_index, positive_index).\"\"\"\n        from cohezion.flume.data_pipeline import ContrastivePairMiner\n\n        tasks = [\n            {\"text\": \"a\", \"group_id\": \"g1\"},\n            {\"text\": \"b\", \"group_id\": \"g1\"},\n        ]\n        miner = ContrastivePairMiner()\n        pairs = miner.mine_pairs(tasks)\n\n        for pair in pairs:\n            assert len(pair) == 2\n            assert isinstance(pair[0], int)\n            assert isinstance(pair[1], int)\n\n\nclass TestTrainingDataPipeline:\n    \"\"\"Test the full data preparation pipeline.\"\"\"\n\n    def test_prepare_returns_embeddings_and_pairs(self):\n mock_provider.embed_batch.return_value = np.random.randn(10, 768).astype(np.float32)\n        mock_provider.embedding_dim = 768\n\n        pipeline = mock_provider.embed_batch.return_value = np.random.randn(20, 768).astype(np.float32)\n        mock_provider.embedding_dim = 768\n\n        pipeline = TrainingDataPipeline(embedding_provider=mock_provider)\n        pipeline.prepare(n_synthetic=20)\n\n        assert mock_provider.embed_batch.called\n"

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/src/cohezion/flume/data_pipeline.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Training data pipeline for FLUME VAE v2.\n\nGenerates synthetic task descriptions across 5 operation types,\nmines contrastive pairs from paraphrase groups, and embeds via\nan EmbeddingProvider.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport itertools\nimport logging\nimport random\nfrom typing import TYPE_CHECKING\n\nimport numpy as np\n\nif TYPE_CHECKING:\n    from cohezion.flume.embedding_provider import EmbeddingProvider\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n# Task templates organized by operation type and paraphrase group.\n# Each group contains semantically equivalent descriptions.\n_TASK_TEMPLATES: dict[str, list[list[str]]] = {\n    \"generate\": [\n        [\"generate a summary report\", \"create a summary report\", \"produce summary report\"],\n        [\"write unit tests\", \"create unit tests\", \"generate test cases\"],\n        [\"generate API documentation\", \"create API docs\", \"produce API reference\"],\n        [\"build a data pipeline\", \"create data pipeline\", \"construct data pipeline\"],\n        [\"generate embeddings for text\", \"create text embeddings\", \"produce vector embeddings\"],\n    ],\n    \"analyze\": [\n        [\"analyze code quality\", \"assess code quality\", \"evaluate code quality\"],\n        [\"analyze performance bottlenecks\", \"identify performance issues\", \"find performance problems\"],\n        [\"review pull request changes\", \"analyze PR diff\", \"evaluate pull request\"],\n        [\"analyze test coverage\", \"assess test coverage\", \"evaluate test coverage gaps\"],\n        [\"analyze error patterns\", \"investigate error trends\", \"examine failure patterns\"],\n    ],\n    \"search\": [\n        [\"search for relevant documentation\", \"find related docs\", \"look up documentation\"],\n        [\"search codebase for pattern\", \"find code matching pattern\", \"grep for code pattern\"],\n        [\"search for similar issues\", \"find related bugs\", \"look up similar problems\"],\n        [\"search vault for decisions\", \"find past decisions\", \"query decision history\"],\n        [\"search for API endpoints\", \"find available endpoints\", \"discover API routes\"],\n    ],\n    \"transform\": [\n        [\"refactor the authentication module\", \"restructure auth module\", \"reorganize authentication code\"],\n        [\"transform data format to JSON\", \"convert data to JSON\", \"serialize data as JSON\"],\n        [\"migrate database schema\", \"update database schema\", \"transform DB schema\"],\n        [\"transform config to YAML\", \"convert configuration to YAML\", \"reformat config as YAML\"],\n        [\"normalize the input data\", \"standardize input format\", \"clean and normalize inputs\"],\n    ],\n    \"persist\": [\n        [\"save checkpoint to disk\", \"write checkpoint file\", \"persist model checkpoint\"],\n        [\"store results in database\", \"save results to DB\", \"persist results in storage\"],\n        [\"cache embeddings locally\", \"store embeddings in cache\", \"persist embedding vectors\"],\n        [\"log experiment results\", \"record experiment outcome\", \"persist experiment data\"],\n        [\"save session state\", \"persist session context\", \"store session snapshot\"],\n    ],\n}\n\n\nclass SyntheticTaskGenerator:\n    \"\"\"Generate synthetic task descriptions from templates.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, seed: int | None = None) -> None:\n        self._rng = random.Random(seed)\n\n    def generate(self, n: int = 5000) -> list[dict[str, str]]:\n        \"\"\"Generate n task descriptions with operation types and group IDs.\"\"\"\n        # Flatten templates into (op_type, group_id, text) triples\n        all_items: list[tuple[str, str, str]] = []\n        for op_type, groups in _TASK_TEMPLATES.items():\n            for g_idx, group in enumerate(groups):\n                group_id = f\"{op_type}_{g_idx}\"\n                for text in group:\n                    all_items.append((op_type, group_id, text))\n\n        tasks: list[dict[str, str]] = []\n        while len(tasks) < n:\n            item = self._rng.choice(all_items)\n            tasks.append({\n                \"text\": item[2],\n                \"op_type\": item[0],\n                \"group_id\": item[1],\n            })\n\n        return tasks[:n]\n\n\nclass ContrastivePairMiner:\n    \"\"\"Mine positive (same-group) pairs for contrastive learning.\"\"\"\n\n    def mine_pairs(self, tasks: list[dict]) -> list[tuple[int, int]]:\n        \"\"\"Return (anchor_idx, positive_idx) pairs from same group.\"\"\"\n        groups: dict[str, list[int]] = {}\n        for idx, t in enumerate(tasks):\n            gid = t[\"group_id\"]\n            groups.setdefault(gid, []).append(idx)\n\n        pairs: list[tuple[int, int]] = []\n        for indices in groups.values():\n            if len(indices) >= 2:\n                for a, b in itertools.combinations(indices, 2):\n                    pairs.append((a, b))\n\n        return pairs\n\n\nclass TrainingDataPipeline:\n    \"\"\"Full data preparation: generate tasks → embed → mine pairs.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, embedding_provider: EmbeddingProvider, seed: int | None = None) -> None:\n        self._provider = embedding_provider\n        self._task_gen = SyntheticTaskGenerator(seed=seed)\n        self._pair_miner = ContrastivePairMiner()\n\n    def prepare(\n        self, n_synthetic: int = 5000, batch_size: int = 50\n    ) -> dict:\n        \"\"\"Generate tasks, embed them, and mine contrastive pairs.\n\n        Returns dict with 'embeddings' (N x dim), 'texts', 'tasks', 'pairs'.\n        \"\"\"\n        tasks = self._task_gen.generate(n=n_synthetic)\n        texts = [t[\"text\"] for t in tasks]\n\n        # Batch embed\n        all_embeddings: list[np.ndarray] = []\n        for i in range(0, len(texts), batch_size):\n            batch = texts[i : i + batch_size]\n            emb = self._provider.embed_batch(batch)\n            all_embeddings.append(emb)\n\n        embeddings = np.concatenate(all_embeddings, axis=0)\n        pairs = self._pair_miner.mine_pairs(tasks)\n\n        logger.info(\n            \"Prepared %d samples (%dD), %d contrastive pairs\",\n            len(texts), embeddings.shape[1], len(pairs),\n        )\n\n        return {\n            \"embeddings\": embeddings,\n            \"texts\": texts,\n            \"tasks\": tasks,\n            \"pairs\": pairs,\n        }\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/tests/flume/test_train_vae_v2.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Tests for FLUME VAE v2 training pipeline.\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport pytest\nimport torch\n\n\nclass TestKLAnnealing:\n    \"\"\"Test KL annealing schedule.\"\"\"\n\n    def test_beta_starts_at_zero(self):\n        \"\"\"Beta should start at 0 at step 0.\"\"\"\n        from cohezion.flume.train_vae import kl_annealing_beta\n\n        assert kl_annealing_beta(step=0, warmup_steps=100, max_beta=0.1) == 0.0\n\n    def test_beta_reaches_max_after_warmup(self):\n        \"\"\"Beta should reach max_beta at warmup_steps.\"\"\"\n        from cohezion.flume.train_vae import kl_annealing_beta\n\n        beta = kl_annealing_beta(step=100, warmup_steps=100, max_beta=0.1)\n        assert abs(beta - 0.1) < 1e-6\n\n    def test_beta_stays_at_max_after_warmup(self):\n        \"\"\"Beta should not exceed max_beta after warmup.\"\"\"\n        from cohezion.flume.train_vae import kl_annealing_beta\n\n        beta = kl_annealing_beta(step=200, warmup_steps=100, max_beta=0.1)\n        assert abs(beta - 0.1) < 1e-6\n\n    def test_beta_linearly_increases(self):\n        \"\"\"Beta should increase linearly during warmup.\"\"\"\n        from cohezion.flume.train_vae import kl_annealing_beta\n\n        beta_half = kl_annealing_beta(step=50, warmup_steps=100, max_beta=0.1)\n        assert abs(beta_half - 0.05) < 1e-6\n\n\nclass TestActiveUnits:\n    \"\"\"Test active unit counting.\"\"\"\n\n    def test_counts_active_units(self):\n        \"\"\"Should count dimensions with variance above threshold.\"\"\"\n        from cohezion.flume.train_vae import count_active_units\n\n        # 128 dims with high variance, 128 with low\n        mu = torch.randn(100, 256)\n        mu[:, 128:] = 0.0  # Kill variance in last 128 dims\n\n        active = count_active_units(mu, threshold=0.01)\n        # First 128 should be active (randn has var ~1)\n        assert active >= 100  # At least 100 of 128 should be active\n\n    def test_all_active_when_varied(self):\n        \"\"\"All units should be active when all dimensions vary.\"\"\"\n        from cohezion.flume.train_vae import count_active_units\n\n        mu = torch.randn(200, 256)\n        active = count_active_units(mu, threshold=0.01)\n        assert active >= 240  # Almost all should be active\n\n\nclass TestVAETrainer:\n    \"\"\"Test the training loop.\"\"\"\n\n    def test_loss_decreases_over_epochs(self):\n        \"\"\"Loss should decrease after a few training epochs on synthetic data.\"\"\"\n        from cohezion.flume.train_vae import VAETrainer\n        from cohezion.flume.vae import FlumeVAE\n\n        torch.manual_seed(42)\n        model = FlumeVAE(input_dim=768, latent_dim=256)\n        data = torch.randn(200, 768)\n\n        trainer = VAETrainer(model, lr=1e-3, max_beta=0.1, warmup_fraction=0.3)\n        history = trainer.train(data, epochs=5, batch_size=32)\n\n        assert history[-1][\"total_loss\"] < history[0][\"total_loss\"]\n\n    def test_checkpoint_save_and_load(self, tmp_path: Path):\n        \"\"\"Checkpoint should save and load correctly.\"\"\"\n        from cohezion.flume.train_vae import VAETrainer\n        from cohezion.flume.vae import FlumeVAE\n\n        torch.manual_seed(42)\n        model = FlumeVAE(input_dim=768, latent_dim=256)\n        trainer = VAETrainer(model, lr=1e-3)\n\n        ckpt_path = tmp_path / \"test_checkpoint.pt\"\n        trainer.save_checkpoint(ckpt_path, epoch=5, metrics={\"loss\": 0.5})\n\n        assert ckpt_path.exists()\n\n        # Load into new model\n        model2 = FlumeVAE(input_dim=768, latent_dim=256)\n        trainer2 = VAETrainer(model2, lr=1e-3)\n        loaded = trainer2.load_checkpoint(ckpt_path)\n\n        assert loaded[\"epoch\"] == 5\n        assert loaded[\"metrics\"][\"loss\"] == 0.5\n\n        # Verify models produce same output\n        x = torch.randn(2, 768)\n        model.eval()\n        model2.eval()\n        with torch.no_grad():\n            out1 = model(x)\n            out2 = model2(x)\n        torch.testing.assert_close(out1[0], out2[0])\n\n    def test_training_records_kl_and_recon(self):\n        \"\"\"Training history should record KL and reconstruction losses.\"\"\"\n        from cohezion.flume.train_vae import VAETrainer\n        from cohezion.flume.vae import FlumeVAE\n\n        torch.manual_seed(42)\n        model = FlumeVAE(input_dim=768, latent_dim=256)\n        data = torch.randn(100, 768)\n\n        trainer = VAETrainer(model, lr=1e-3)\n        history = trainer.train(data, epochs=2, batch_size=32)\n\n        for entry in history:\n            assert \"total_loss\" in entry\n            assert \"recon_loss\" in entry\n            assert \"kl_loss\" in entry\n            assert \"beta\" in entry\n\n    def test_grad_clipping_applied(self):\n        \"\"\"Gradient clipping should prevent explosion.\"\"\"\n        from cohezion.flume.train_vae import VAETrainer\n        from cohezion.flume.vae import FlumeVAE\n\n        model = FlumeVAE(input_dim=768, latent_dim=256)\n        trainer = VAETrainer(model, lr=1e-3, grad_clip=1.0)\n\n        # Train one step with large input to trigger large gradients\n        data = torch.randn(32, 768) * 100\n        history = trainer.train(data, epochs=1, batch_size=32)\n\n        # Should complete without NaN\n        assert not any(np.isnan(h[\"total_loss\"]) for h in history)\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/src/cohezion/flume/train_vae.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Training pipeline for FLUME VAE v2.\n\nFeatures:\n  - KL annealing (linear warmup)\n  - Free-bits per dimension\n  - Gradient clipping\n  - Checkpoint save/load\n  - Active unit monitoring\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport logging\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport torch\nimport torch.nn as nn\nfrom torch.utils.data import DataLoader, TensorDataset\n\nfrom cohezion.flume.vae import FlumeVAE, flume_vae_loss\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n\ndef kl_annealing_beta(step: int, warmup_steps: int, max_beta: float) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Linear KL annealing: 0 → max_beta over warmup_steps.\"\"\"\n    if warmup_steps <= 0:\n        return max_beta\n    return min(max_beta, max_beta * step / warmup_steps)\n\n\ndef count_active_units(mu: torch.Tensor, threshold: float = 0.01) -> int:\n    \"\"\"Count latent dimensions with variance above threshold across samples.\"\"\"\n    var_per_dim = mu.var(dim=0)\n    return int((var_per_dim > threshold).sum().item())\n\n\nclass VAETrainer:\n    \"\"\"Train FLUME VAE with KL annealing and monitoring.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        model: FlumeVAE,\n        lr: float = 1e-3,\n        weight_decay: float = 1e-4,\n        max_beta: float = 0.1,\n        warmup_fraction: float = 0.3,\n        free_bits: float = 0.125,\n        lambda_coherence: float = 0.01,\n        grad_clip: float = 1.0,\n    ) -> None:\n        self.model = model\n        self.max_beta = max_beta\n        self.warmup_fraction = warmup_fraction\n        self.free_bits = free_bits\n        self.lambda_coherence = lambda_coherence\n        self.grad_clip = grad_clip\n\n        self.optimizer = torch.optim.AdamW(\n            model.parameters(), lr=lr, weight_decay=weight_decay\n        )\n\n    def train(\n        self,\n        data: torch.Tensor,\n        epochs: int = 50,\n        batch_size: int = 64,\n    ) -> list[dict[str, float]]:\n        \"\"\"Train VAE on embedded data. Returns per-epoch metrics.\"\"\"\n        dataset = TensorDataset(data)\n        loader = DataLoader(dataset, batch_size=batch_size, shuffle=True)\n\n        total_steps = epochs * len(loader)\n        warmup_steps = int(total_steps * self.warmup_fraction)\n\n        history: list[dict[str, float]] = []\n        global_step = 0\n\n        self.model.train()\n        for epoch in range(epochs):\n            epoch_losses: dict[str, list[float]] = {\n                \"total_loss\": [], \"recon_loss\": [], \"kl_loss\": [],\n                \"coherence_loss\": [],\n            }\n            current_beta = 0.0\n\n            for (batch,) in loader:\n                current_beta = kl_annealing_beta(\n                    global_step, warmup_steps, self.max_beta\n                )\n\n                recon, mu, logvar, z = self.model(batch)\n                losses = flume_vae_loss(\n                    batch, recon, mu, logvar,\n                    beta=current_beta,\n                    free_bits=self.free_bits,\n                    lambda_coherence=self.lambda_coherence,\n                )\n\n                self.optimizer.zero_grad()\n                losses[\"total_loss\"].backward()\n\n                if self.grad_clip > 0:\n                    nn.utils.clip_grad_norm_(\n                        self.model.parameters(), self.grad_clip\n                    )\n\n                self.optimizer.step()\n                global_step += 1\n\n                for k in epoch_losses:\n                    epoch_losses[k].append(losses[k].item())\n\n            avg = {k: sum(v) / len(v) for k, v in epoch_losses.items()}\n            avg[\"beta\"] = current_beta\n            avg[\"epoch\"] = epoch\n            history.append(avg)\n\n            if (epoch + 1) % 10 == 0 or epoch == 0:\n                logger.info(\n                    \"Epoch %d: loss=%.4f recon=%.4f kl=%.4f β=%.4f\",\n                    epoch, avg[\"total_loss\"], avg[\"recon_loss\"],\n                    avg[\"kl_loss\"], current_beta,\n                )\n\n        return history\n\n    def save_checkpoint(\n        self, path: Path, epoch: int, metrics: dict | None = None\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Save model + optimizer state.\"\"\"\n        torch.save(\n            {\n                \"model_state_dict\": self.model.state_dict(),\n                \"optimizer_state_dict\": self.optimizer.state_dict(),\n                \"epoch\": epoch,\n                \"metrics\": metrics or {},\n                \"config\": {\n                    \"input_dim\": self.model.input_dim,\n                    \"latent_dim\": self.model.latent_dim,\n                    \"max_beta\": self.max_beta,\n                    \"free_bits\": self.free_bits,\n                },\n            },\n            path,\n        )\n        logger.info(\"Saved checkpoint to %s (epoch %d)\", path, epoch)\n\n    def load_checkpoint(self, path: Path) -> dict:\n        \"\"\"Load checkpoint into current model/optimizer.\"\"\"\n        ckpt = torch.load(path, map_location=\"cpu\", weights_only=True)\n        self.model.load_state_dict(ckpt[\"model_state_dict\"])\n        self.optimizer.load_state_dict(ckpt[\"optimizer_state_dict\"])\n        logger.info(\"Loaded checkpoint from %s (epoch %d)\", path, ckpt[\"epoch\"])\n        return {\"epoch\": ckpt[\"epoch\"], \"metrics\": ckpt[\"metrics\"], \"config\": ckpt[\"config\"]}\n"
}
