   206→
   207→class SemanticCache:
   208→    """L2 cache with semantic fuzzy matching using embeddings.
   209→
   210→    Bridges L1 (exact) and L3 (persistent) caches with fuzzy matching.
   211→    Enables cache hits on semantically similar (but not identical) prompts.
   212→
   213→    Uses FLUME VAE embeddings by default (256D learned representations)
   214→    with hash-based fallback for resilience. Automatically adjusts similarity
   215→    threshold based on embedding model type.
   216→
   217→    Attributes:
   218→        similarity_threshold: Minimum cosine similarity for hit (default: 0.88 for VAE, 0.25 for hash)
   219→        embedding_dim: Dimension of embedding vectors (256 for VAE, 384 for hash)
   220→        max_entries: Maximum cached embeddings in memory
   221→        embedding_model: Model for generating embeddings (FlumeVAEEmbeddingModel by default)
   222→    """
   223→
   224→    def __init__(
   225→        self,
   226→        similarity_threshold: float | None = None,
   227→        embedding_dim: int = 256,
   228→        max_entries: int = 1000,
   229→        embedding_model: EmbeddingModel | None = None,
   230→        cache_dir: str | Path = "data/cache",
   231→    ):
   232→        """Initialize semantic cache.
   233→
   234→        Args:
   235→            similarity_threshold: Min cosine similarity for hit (0-1).
   236→                If None, defaults to 0.88 for FLUME VAE, 0.25 for hash.
   237→            embedding_dim: Embedding vector dimension (256 for VAE, 384 for hash)
   238→            max_entries: Max embeddings to keep in memory
   239→            embedding_model: Custom embedding model (uses FlumeVAEEmbeddingModel if None)
   240→            cache_dir: Directory for optional persistence
   241→
   242→        Note:
   243→            Default embedding model is FlumeVAEEmbeddingModel which uses FLUME VAE
   244→            with automatic fallback to hash-based embeddings. This provides 50×+  # noqa: RUF002
   245→            better discrimination for paraphrase matching.
   246→        """
   247→        self.embedding_dim = embedding_dim
   248→        self.max_entries = max_entries
   249→        self.cache_dir = Path(cache_dir)
   250→
   251→        # Embedding model (lazy initialized) - prefer FLUME VAE
   252→        if embedding_model is None:
   253→            # Try FLUME VAE first (production), fallback to hash-based
   254→            self._embedding_model = FlumeVAEEmbeddingModel()
   255→            # FLUME VAE: 256D, high threshold (0.88) for real semantic similarity
   256→            # Hash-based fallback: 256D, low threshold (0.25) for pragmatic matching
   257→            if similarity_threshold is None:
   258→                similarity_threshold = 0.88  # For FLUME VAE embeddings
   259→        else:
   260→            self._embedding_model = embedding_model
   261→            # Custom model: use provided threshold or default to 0.88
   262→            if similarity_threshold is None:
   263→                similarity_threshold = 0.88
   264→
   265→        self.similarity_threshold = similarity_threshold
   266→        self._model_ready = False
   267→
   268→        # In-memory embedding store: key → (embedding, cached_value)
   269→        self._embedding_cache: dict[str, tuple[list[float], Any]] = {}
   270→        self._access_order: OrderedDict[str, None] = OrderedDict()
   271→
   272→        # Thread safety
   273→        self._lock = threading.RLock()
   274→
   275→        # Statistics
   276→        self._stats = {
   277→            "queries": 0,
   278→            "hits": 0,
   279→            "misses": 0,
   280→            "embedding_errors": 0,
   281→        }
   282→
   283→    async def get(self, prompt: str, system: str = "") -> SemanticCacheHit | None:
   284→        """Find semantically similar cached response.
   285→
   286→        Args:
   287→            prompt: User prompt to match
   288→            system: System prompt (included in similarity)
   289→
   290→        Returns:
   291→            SemanticCacheHit with confidence if found, None otherwise
   292→        """
   293→        with self._lock:
   294→            self._stats["queries"] += 1
   295→
   296→            if not self._embedding_cache:
   297→                self._stats["misses"] += 1
   298→                return None
   299→
   300→            # Generate embedding for query
   301→            try:
   302→                query_embedding = await self._generate_embedding(prompt, system)
   303→            except Exception as e:
   304→                logger.debug(f"Failed to generate query embedding: {e}")
   305→                self._stats["embedding_errors"] += 1
   306→                self._stats["misses"] += 1
   307→                return None
   308→
   309→            # Find most similar cached embedding
   310→            best_hit = None
   311→            best_similarity = -1.0
   312→
   313→            for key, (cached_embedding, cached_value) in self._embedding_cache.items():
   314→                similarity = self._cosine_similarity(query_embedding, cached_embedding)
   315→
   316→                if similarity > best_similarity and similarity >= self.similarity_threshold:
   317→                    best_similarity = similarity
   318→                    best_hit = SemanticCacheHit(
   319→                        value=cached_value,
   320→                        confidence=float(best_similarity),
   321→                        key=key,
   322→                    )
   323→
   324→            if best_hit:
   325→                self._stats["hits"] += 1
   326→                # Move to end (recently used)
   327→                self._access_order.move_to_end(key)
   328→                return best_hit
   329→
   330→            self._stats["misses"] += 1
   331→            return None
   332→
   333→    async def put(
   334→        self,
   335→        prompt: str,
   336→        system: str,
   337→        model: str,
   338→        value: Any,
   339→        cache_key: str | None = None,
   340→    ) -> None:
   341→        """Store response with semantic embedding.
   342→
   343→        Args:
   344→            prompt: User prompt
   345→            system: System prompt
   346→            model: Model used (included in embedding)
   347→            value: Response value to cache
   348→            cache_key: Original L1 cache key (for deduplication)
   349→        """
   350→        with self._lock:
   351→            # Generate embedding
   352→            try:
   353→                embedding = await self._generate_embedding(prompt, system)
   354→            except Exception as e:
   355→                logger.debug(f"Failed to generate embedding for storage: {e}")
   356→                self._stats["embedding_errors"] += 1
   357→                return
   358→
   359→            # Use provided key or generate one
   360→            if cache_key is None:
   361→                cache_key = f"{model}_{hash((prompt, system)) % 1000000}"
   362→
   363→            # Store in cache
   364→            self._embedding_cache[cache_key] = (embedding, value)
   365→            self._access_order[cache_key] = None
   366→
   367→            # Evict if necessary
   368→            if len(self._embedding_cache) > self.max_entries:
   369→                self._evict_lru()
   370→
   371→    async def _generate_embedding(self, prompt: str, system: str) -> list[float]:
   372→        """Generate embedding for prompt+system.
   373→
   374→        Args:
   375→            prompt: User prompt
   376→            system: System prompt
   377→
   378→        Returns:
   379→            Embedding vector as list[float]
   380→        """
   381→        # Combine prompt and system for richer embedding
   382→        combined_text = f"{system}\n{prompt}" if system else prompt
   383→
   384→        result = await self._embedding_model.encode(combined_text)
   385→        return result.embedding
   386→
   387→    def _cosine_similarity(self, vec1: list[float], vec2: list[float]) -> float:
   388→        """Calculate cosine similarity between two vectors.
   389→
   390→        Args:
   391→            vec1: First vector
   392→            vec2: Second vector
   393→
   394→        Returns:
   395→            Cosine similarity (0 to 1)
   396→        """
   397→        try:
   398→            arr1 = np.array(vec1, dtype=np.float32)
   399→            arr2 = np.array(vec2, dtype=np.float32)
```

     1→#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2→"""CLI for training the FLUME VAE autoencoder.
     3→
     4→Usage:
     5→    uv run python scripts/train_flume.py
     6→    uv run python scripts/train_flume.py --epochs 100 --lr 5e-4
     7→    uv run python scripts/train_flume.py --synthetic --epochs 20
     8→"""
     9→
    10→from __future__ import annotations
    11→
    12→import argparse
    13→import json
    14→import logging
    15→import sys
    16→
    17→
    18→logging.basicConfig(
    19→    level=logging.INFO,
    20→    format="%(asctime)s | %(levelname)s | %(message)s",
    21→    datefmt="%H:%M:%S",
    22→)
    23→
    24→
    25→def main() -> int:
    26→    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Train FLUME VAE autoencoder")
    27→    parser.add_argument("--epochs", type=int, default=50)
    28→    parser.add_argument("--batch-size", type=int, default=64)
    29→    parser.add_argument("--lr", type=float, default=1e-3)
    30→    parser.add_argument("--z-dim", type=int, default=256)
    31→    parser.add_argument("--kl-weight", type=float, default=0.1)
    32→    parser.add_argument("--coherence-weight", type=float, default=0.05)
    33→    parser.add_argument("--data-dir", default="data/mass_sim/artifacts")
    34→    parser.add_argument("--checkpoint-dir", default="data/flume/checkpoints")
    35→    parser.add_argument("--synthetic", action="store_true", help="Use synthetic data")
    36→    parser.add_argument("--n-samples", type=int, default=10000)
    37→
    38→    args = parser.parse_args()
    39→
    40→    from cohezion.flume.training import FlumeVAETrainer, TrainConfig
    41→
    42→    config = TrainConfig(
    43→        z_dim=args.z_dim,
    44→        batch_size=args.batch_size,
    45→        epochs=args.epochs,
    46→        lr=args.lr,
    47→        kl_weight=args.kl_weight,
    48→        coherence_weight=args.coherence_weight,
    49→        checkpoint_dir=args.checkpoint_dir,
    50→        data_dir=args.data_dir,
    51→    )
    52→
    53→    trainer = FlumeVAETrainer(config)
    54→
    55→    dataset = None
    56→    if args.synthetic:
    57→        from cohezion.flume.dataset import SyntheticFlumeDataset
    58→
    59→        dataset = SyntheticFlumeDataset(n_samples=args.n_samples, z_dim=args.z_dim)
    60→
    61→    metrics = trainer.train(dataset=dataset)
    62→
    63→    # Save metrics summary
    64→    from pathlib import Path
    65→
    66→    out = Path(args.checkpoint_dir) / "training_metrics.json"
    67→    with open(out, "w") as f:
    68→        json.dump(metrics, f, indent=2, default=str)
    69→    print(f"\nTraining complete. Metrics saved to {out}")
    70→
    71→    final = metrics[-1]
    72→    print(f"Final MSE: {final['mse']:.4f} | KL: {final['kl']:.4f} | Total: {final['total']:.4f}")
    73→    return 0
    74→
    75→
    76→if __name__ == "__main__":
    77→    sys.exit(main())
    78→

     1→"""Collect execution experiences from Parquet, SurrealDB, and vault sources.
     2→
     3→Three tiers, each non-blocking:
     4→  Tier 1: Parquet journey shards  (data/journeys/shard_*.parquet)
     5→  Tier 2: SurrealDB mission_journey table
     6→  Tier 3: Vault experiment JSON files

     8→
     9→from __future__ import annotations
    10→
    11→import json
    12→import logging
    13→from pathlib import Path
    14→
    15→import numpy as np
    16→
    17→
    18→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    19→
    20→# Default paths
    21→_PARQUET_DIR = Path("data/journeys")
    22→_VAULT_DIR = Path.home() / "vaults" / "cohezion-vault" / "experiments"
    23→
    24→
    25→class ExperienceCollector:
    26→    """Gather execution experiences from all available data sources.
    27→
    28→    Parameters
    29→    ----------
    30→    parquet_dir : Path
    31→        Directory containing ``shard_*.parquet`` files.
    32→    vault_dir : Path
    33→        Directory containing vault experiment JSON files.
    34→    """
    35→
    36→    def __init__(
    37→        self,
    38→        parquet_dir: Path | str = _PARQUET_DIR,
    39→        vault_dir: Path | str = _VAULT_DIR,
    40→    ) -> None:
    41→        self.parquet_dir = Path(parquet_dir)
    42→        self.vault_dir = Path(vault_dir)
    43→
    44→    def collect_all(self, max_samples: int = 100_000) -> list[dict]:
    45→        """Collect experiences from all tiers.
    46→
    47→        Returns a list of normalized experience dicts with keys matching
    48→        the ExperienceEncoder schema.
    49→        """
    50→        records: list[dict] = []
    51→        records.extend(self._collect_parquet(max_samples))
    52→        if len(records) < max_samples:
    53→            records.extend(self._collect_surreal(max_samples - len(records)))
    54→        if len(records) < max_samples:
    55→            records.extend(self._collect_vault(max_samples - len(records)))
    56→        logger.info("Collected %d total experience records", len(records))
    57→        return records[:max_samples]
    58→
    59→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    60→    # Tier 1: Parquet shards
```

     1→# FLUME VAE: Red Flags → Green Lights
     2→
     3→## Core Principles
     4→
     5→### 1. Learn Before You Burn
     6→
     7→**No destructive operations without extracting knowledge first.** Before removing, replacing, or deprecating any code, data, or artifact:
     8→
     9→1. **Identify patterns** — What good ideas exist in the code being removed? What design decisions were sound even if the implementation failed?
    10→2. **Identify anti-patterns** — What went wrong? What assumptions were incorrect? What traps should future work avoid?
    11→3. **Store in vault** — Log all patterns via `vault_extract_pattern` and anti-patterns via `vault_log_decision` to the MCP vault. Knowledge compounds; deleted code doesn't.
    12→4. **Only then proceed** with removal.
    13→
    14→This applies to every phase of this spec, not just Sprint 0. When Phase 2 replaces Phase 1 components, the same discipline applies.
    15→
    16→### 2. Test-Driven Development (Red → Green → Refactor)
    17→
    18→**Every feature starts with a failing test.** No production code without seeing RED first.
    19→
    20→| Step | Action | Verification |
    21→|------|--------|-------------|
    22→| **RED** | Write a test that describes the desired behavior. Run it. Watch it fail. | Test fails for the *right reason* (missing feature, not syntax error) |
    23→| **GREEN** | Write the minimum code to make the test pass. Nothing more. | Test passes. All existing tests still pass. |
    24→| **REFACTOR** | Improve code quality while keeping tests green. | All tests still pass after changes. |
    25→
    26→**Applied to FLUME:**
    27→- Each VAE component (encoder, decoder, loss, training loop) gets its test FIRST
    28→- Evaluation metrics get tests that verify known inputs produce expected outputs BEFORE implementation
    29→- Integration points (SemanticCache, JourneyTracker) get contract tests BEFORE wiring
    30→
    31→**Recovery rule:** If production code was written first (hook fires), write the test immediately — don't revert working code, but apply TDD properly going forward.
    32→
    33→### 3. Compound Engineering
    34→
    35→**Every feature makes future features easier.** The compound loop applies to this spec:
    36→
    37→```
    38→Implement feature → Validate manually → Write 5 tests → Extract learning → Refine skill
    39→```
    40→
    41→| Principle | Application in This Spec |
    42→|-----------|-------------------------|
    43→| **Knowledge compounds** | Every anti-pattern logged to vault prevents re-occurrence. Every pattern extracted is reusable. |
    44→| **Build on proven templates** | New EmbeddingProvider follows existing `ExperienceCollector` multi-tier fallback pattern. New VAE loss follows existing `FlumeVAETrainer` structure (improved, not reinvented). |
    45→| **Experience-guided execution** | Query vault for prior learnings before each sprint (lesson-37 validates this). |
    46→| **Skill refinement loop** | After Sprint 3 evaluation, log results as vault experiment. Learnings flow back into Sprint 4 decisions. |
    47→| **Honest metrics** | Report actual numbers (98.8% beats inflated 100%). Green-light criteria are pass/fail, not "close enough." |
    48→
    49→### 4. Context Awareness
    50→
```

Query with paraphrases (from same template group) — measure if correct original is retrieved\n  5. Query with unrelated tasks — measure false positive rate\n- Metrics: Precision (correct hits / total hits), Recall (correct hits / total paraphrases), F1\n- Compare at the SemanticCache's default thresholds (0.88 for FLUME, 0.25 for hash)\n- This is a sync benchmark — doesn't need the async SemanticCache. **Key observations:**

The plan mentions `--load-data` (Task 2) but no `--load-checkpoint` flag for Task 4's evaluation-only mode. This is a gap.

4. **Task 5 says "sync benchmark" but SemanticCache is async.** The plan notes this correctly and says to use cosine similarity directly on numpy arrays. Good.

5. **Task 6 modifies `DEFAULT_MODEL_PATH` but data files (.npz, .pt) are in `.gitignore` territory.** The plan notes 15 MB for the training data. Should clarify that data/flume should be gitignored or use git-lfs.

6. **Missing `--load-checkpoint` flag.** Task 4's verify command assumes `--epochs 0 --evaluate` will load the trained model, but the plan doesn't define a `--load-checkpoint` flag. The script would need to know where the trained checkpoint is to load it for evaluation-only.

**Plan Verification Findings**

The plan is well-structured and covers all the user's requirements. I found a few issues:

1. **should_fix: Missing `--load-checkpoint` CLI flag.** Task 4's verification command (`--epochs 0 --evaluate --checkpoint-dir`) assumes the script can load a trained checkpoint for evaluation-only mode, but the Task 1 CLI spec doesn't define a `--load-checkpoint` flag. The script needs to load the model state from `flume_vae_v2.pt` before evaluating. Without this, `--epochs 0 --evaluate` would evaluate a randomly initialized model.

2. **should_fix: Inconsistent red-flag count.** The Summary says "validates all 6 red-flag criteria" but Task 4 lists only 5 metrics (KL, active units, cos_sim, P@1, Spearman rho). The evaluate_vae.py also computes exactly 5 metrics. Should say "5" not "6".

3. **suggestion: Data file storage clarification.** Task 2 generates ~15 MB of training data at `data/flume/training/bootstrap_v2.npz`. The plan should note whether `data/flume/training/` is gitignored (it should be, per the data storage architecture). The checkpoint at `data/flume/checkpoints/` also needs the same consideration.

4. **suggestion: `--epochs 0` edge case.** The VAETrainer.train() with epochs=0 returns an empty list (no history). Task 2's verify command uses `--epochs 0` to generate data only. Task 4's verify also uses `--epochs 0`. The CLI script needs explicit handling for this: skip training entirely when epochs=0 rather than calling train() with 0 epochs.

The plan correctly covers: CLI entry point, bootstrap data generation, VAE training, evaluation against all green-light criteria (KL>0.1, active_units>=128, cos_sim>0.95, P@1>0.85, rho>0.90), SemanticCache A/B comparison, and vault logging. Task dependencies are correct. Verification commands are concrete and runnable (with the checkpoint loading fix).

   207→**Objective:** Compare FLUME VAE embeddings vs hash-based embeddings on a benchmark set of paraphrased queries. Measure precision-recall for cache hit detection.

   209→**Dependencies:** Task 3
   210→
   211→**Files:**
   212→
   213→- Create: `scripts/benchmark_semantic_cache.py`
   214→- Test: `tests/flume/test_cache_ablation.py`
   215→
   216→**Key Decisions / Notes:**
   217→
   218→- Benchmark protocol:
   219→  1. Generate 100 task descriptions from SyntheticTaskGenerator
   220→  2. For each, embed with both FLUME VAE (via trained checkpoint) and hash (SHA-256)
   221→  3. Insert originals into respective caches
   222→  4. Query with paraphrases (from same template group) — measure if correct original is retrieved
   223→  5. Query with unrelated tasks — measure false positive rate
   224→- Metrics: Precision (correct hits / total hits), Recall (correct hits / total paraphrases), F1
   225→- Compare at the SemanticCache's default thresholds (0.88 for FLUME, 0.25 for hash)
   226→- This is a sync benchmark — doesn't need the async SemanticCache. Use cosine similarity directly on numpy arrays
   227→
   228→**Definition of Done:**
   229→
   230→- [ ] Benchmark script runs end-to-end
   231→- [ ] FLUME precision-recall dominates hash baseline (higher F1)
   232→- [ ] Results printed as comparison table
   233→- [ ] Test file validates the benchmark logic with known inputs
   234→
   235→**Verify:**
   236→
   237→- `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_cache_ablation.py -q`
   238→- `uv run python scripts/benchmark_semantic_cache.py --checkpoint data/flume/checkpoints/flume_vae_v2.pt`
   239→
   240→### Task 6: Log Results to Vault and Update Checkpoint
   241→
   242→**Objective:** Log training results, evaluation metrics, and A/B comparison as vault experiments. Save final v2 checkpoint as the default model path.

   250→
   251→**Key Decisions / Notes:**
   252→
   253→- Log to vault via MCP: `vault_log_experiment` with training metrics, evaluation results, and A/B comparison
   254→- Update `FlumeVAEEncoder.DEFAULT_MODEL_PATH` from `flume_vae_ep50.pt` to `flume_vae_v2.pt`
   255→- Ensure existing tests still pass with the path change (tests mock the encoder, so path is irrelevant)
   256→- Add entry to MISSION_JOURNAL about training completion
   257→
   258→**Definition of Done:**
   259→
   260→- [ ] Vault experiment logged with all metrics
   261→- [ ] DEFAULT_MODEL_PATH updated to v2 checkpoint
   262→- [ ] All 93+ FLUME tests still pass
   263→- [ ] MISSION_JOURNAL updated with training results summary
   264→

   268→- `grep DEFAULT_MODEL_PATH src/cohezion/flume/vae_encoder.py` — shows `flume_vae_v2.pt`
   269→
   270→## Testing Strategy
   271→
   272→- **Unit tests:** Task 1 tests CLI argument parsing and main function with mocked pipeline. Task 5 tests benchmark logic with known embeddings.
   273→- **Integration tests:** Tasks 2-4 are integration tests by nature — they run real Ollama embeddings and real training. These run via the CLI script, not pytest.
   274→- **Manual verification:** Run `scripts/train_flume_v2.py` end-to-end and inspect output table.
   275→
   276→## Risks and Mitigations
   277→
   278→| Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigation |
   279→|------|-----------|--------|------------|
   280→| Ollama nomic-embed-text API slow for 5K samples | Medium | Medium | Use CachedEmbeddingProvider + batch API (50 at a time). Fall back to fewer samples (2000) if >10min |
   281→| Green-light criteria not met | Medium | High | If reconstruction cos_sim < 0.95: try more epochs (100) or lower dropout. If P@1 < 0.85: add more diverse paraphrase templates. Log partial results to vault for Phase 2 iteration |
   282→| CPU training too slow | Low | Low | 2.1M params on 5K samples for 50 epochs is lightweight. Should complete in 2-5 minutes |
   283→| Checkpoint format breaks existing tests | Low | Medium | Tests mock the encoder — path changes don't affect them. Run full test suite to verify |

6. **Log to vault + update paths** — Vault experiment, update DEFAULT_MODEL_PATH, journal entry

  "plan": "# FLUME VAE: Red Flags → Green Lights\n\n## Core Principles\n\n### 1. Learn Before You Burn\n\n**No destructive operations without extracting knowledge first.** Before removing, replacing, or deprecating any code, data, or artifact:\n\n1. **Identify patterns** — What good ideas exist in the code being removed? What design decisions were sound even if the implementation failed?\n2. **Identify anti-patterns** — What went wrong? What assumptions were incorrect? What traps should future work avoid?\n3. **Store in vault** — Log all patterns via `vault_extract_pattern` and anti-patterns via `vault_log_decision` to the MCP vault. Knowledge compounds; deleted code doesn't.\n4. **Only then proceed** with removal.\n\nThis applies to every phase of this spec, not just Sprint 0. When Phase 2 replaces Phase 1 components, the same discipline applies.\n\n### 2. Test-Driven Development (Red → Green → Refactor)\n\n**Every feature starts with a failing test.** No production code without seeing RED first.\n\n| Step | Action | Verification |\n|------|--------|-------------|\n| **RED** | Write a test that describes the desired behavior. Run it. Watch it fail. | Test fails for the *right reason* (missing feature, not syntax error) |\n| **GREEN** | Write the minimum code to make the test pass. Nothing more. | Test passes. All existing tests still pass. |\n| **REFACTOR** | Improve code quality while keeping tests green. | All tests still pass after changes. |\n\n**Applied to FLUME:**\n- Each VAE component (encoder, decoder, loss, training loop) gets its test FIRST\n- Evaluation metrics get tests that verify known inputs produce expected outputs BEFORE implementation\n- Integration points (SemanticCache, JourneyTracker) get contract tests BEFORE wiring\n\n**Recovery rule:** If production code was written first (hook fires), write the test immediately — don't revert working code, but apply TDD properly going forward.\n\n### 3. Compound Engineering\n\n**Every feature makes future features easier.** The compound loop applies to this spec:\n\n```\nImplement feature → Validate manually → Write 5 tests → Extract learning → Refine skill\n```\n\n| Principle | Application in This Spec |\n|-----------|-------------------------|\n| **Knowledge compounds** | Every anti-pattern logged to vault prevents re-occurrence. Every pattern extracted is reusable. |\n| **Build on proven templates** | New EmbeddingProvider follows existing `ExperienceCollector` multi-tier fallback pattern. New VAE loss follows existing `FlumeVAETrainer` structure (improved, not reinvented). |\n| **Experience-guided execution** | Query vault for prior learnings before each sprint (lesson-37 validates this). |\n| **Skill refinement loop** | After Sprint 3 evaluation, log results as vault experiment. Learnings flow back into Sprint 4 decisions. |\n| **Honest metrics** | Report actual numbers (98.8% beats inflated 100%). Green-light criteria are pass/fail, not \"close enough.\" |\n\n### 4. Context Awareness\n\n**Every session starts informed.** No cold starts.\n\n| Checkpoint | Action |\n|------------|--------|\n| **Sprint start** | Query vault: `vault_find_relevant_context(query=\"FLUME VAE training\")`. Read continuation file if exists. Check existing experiment notes. |\n| **Before building** | Query Context7 for library docs (PyTorch VAE patterns, nomic-embed-text API). Don't assume — verify. |\n| **Mid-sprint** | Check `cz context --json`. If >80%, prepare handoff. Write continuation with EXACT state. |\n| **Sprint end** | Log to vault: decisions made, metrics achieved, patterns discovered. Next session starts informed. |\n\n**Overnight simulation data** (5.5M trajectories) is a context asset. Log its characteristics to vault so Phase 2 sessions can find it without re-discovering.\n\n### 5. Token Efficiency\n\n**Budget tokens like money.** Based on CLAUDE.md token budgets:\n\n| Task Type | Budget | Pattern |\n|-----------|--------|---------|\n| Implement 1 feature | 500-1,500 tokens | Copy template → code → manual test → 5 tests |\n| Research + implement | 2,000-3,000 tokens | Quick survey → proof of concept → tests |\n| Single test debug | 200-500 tokens | Add print → run → check → fix → verify |\n\n**Anti-patterns to avoid:**\n- Research-first (5,000-10,000 tokens for no implementation)\n- Infrastructure for products that don't exist (8,000+ tokens)\n- Running full test suite repeatedly (use `-k` to target specific tests)\n\n**Efficiency tactics for this spec:**\n- Use `uv run pytest tests/flume/ -k \"test_vae\"` not `uv run pytest tests/ -q` during development\n- Cache Ollama embeddings in Sprint 1 LRU cache — avoid redundant API calls during training\n- Batch embed texts (50 at a time) instead of one-by-one\n- Mock Ollama in tests — never call live Ollama in unit tests\n- Write narrow tests (one behavior per test) — faster to run, faster to debug\n\n### 6. Evidence Over Assumptions\n\nEvery red flag became a red flag because someone assumed something worked without checking. Every claim in this spec has a concrete verification command. If you can't run a command to prove it, you can't claim it.\n\n### 7. Phased with Hard Gates\n\nNo Phase 2 work begins until ALL Phase 1 green-light criteria are met with evidence. \"Probably fine\" is not a gate-pass.\n\n---\n\n## Context\n\nThe FLUME investigation (2026-02-23) identified 6 red flags — critical unknowns that determine whether FLUME is a viable research artifact or an architectural dead end. Codebase exploration revealed the situation is worse than the investigation assumed: **the system the investigation analyzed doesn't actually exist.** Two incompatible VAE implementations, a broken data pipeline training on random noise, and zero integration with agent behavior mean the red flags aren't \"unknowns to measure\" — they're **architectural problems to solve.**\n\nThis plan starts by freeing system resources (7 GB RAM from zombie processes, 7.4 GB disk from stale artifacts, 2,000+ lines of dead code), then rebuilds FLUME from first principles in two phases, turning each red flag green with concrete, verifiable criteria. It also maps FLUME's capabilities to the Anthropic Universes team Research Engineer role to validate strategic alignment.\n\n---\n\n## Sprint 0: Free RAM + Extract Knowledge\n\nNo file deletions needed — plenty of disk space. Focus on reclaiming RAM and extracting knowledge from existing code before building new.\n\n### Physical Resources\n\n| Resource | What | Recovery | Command |\n|----------|------|----------|---------|\n| **7 GB RAM** | 12 zombie pytest processes running 25+ hours | Kill all | `kill 286767 286775 288864 288872 290708 290714 290718 293426 293432 293435 294452 294457` |\n\n### Prerequisite: Install nomic-embed-text\n\n```bash\nollama pull nomic-embed-text\n```\n\n### Valuable Data Discovered (preserved, not deleted)\n\n| Data | Size | Value |\n|------|------|-------|\n| `data/overnight/journeys.jsonl` | 3.2 GB, 5.5M records | Real 12D simulation trajectories — Phase 2 training data |\n| `data/overnight/checkpoints/` | 200 JSON files | Simulation convergence history (coherence 0.568 → 0.613) |\n| `data/flume/checkpoints/flume_vae_ep*.pt` | 4 checkpoints | Noise-trained model weights — reference for comparison |\n| `data/surrealdb-old/` | 28 MB | Old SurrealDB instance — may contain queryable journey data |\n\n### Sprint 0 Verification\n\n```bash\nfree -h                                # Confirm RAM recovered\npython -c \"from cohezion.flume import FlumeVAEEncoder\"  # Core import works\n```\n\n### Knowledge Extraction (BEFORE any deletions)\n\n**Rule: No destructive operations without learning.** Before removing any code, extract patterns and anti-patterns into the MCP vault. This preserves institutional knowledge for future sessions.\n\n**Anti-patterns to document (via `vault_log_decision` / `vault_extract_pattern`):**\n\n| Anti-Pattern | Source File | Lesson |\n|-------------|------------|--------|\n| Dual VAE architecture | `autoencoder.py` + `training.py` | Two models operating on different input spaces creates integration debt that compounds silently. One coherent model > two partial ones. |\n| Training on random noise | `dataset.py` (SyntheticFlumeDataset) | VAE trained on N(0.5, 0.15) learns Gaussian structure, not semantic structure. Synthetic data must have the *kind* of structure you want the model to learn. |\n| SHA-256 as semantic embedding | `vae_encoder.py` + `experience_encoder.py` | Cryptographic hashes destroy semantic similarity. \"deploy API\" and \"API deployment\" hash to completely unrelated 256D vectors. Never use hash functions where semantic distance matters. |\n| Hash-based journey tracking | `journey_tracker.py` | Deterministic text hashing for 12D trajectories means semantically identical tasks map to different points. Observable AI requires *meaningful* representations, not arbitrary ones. |\n| Disconnected modules | morphospace, bioelectric, lcsp, etc. | 1,353 lines of code with zero consumers = pure maintenance burden. If a module has no imports, it's not \"future work\" — it's dead weight. Enforce \"no orphan modules\" policy. |\n| HIHO coherence loss on batch mean | `training.py` line 118-120 | Penalizing `mean(μ) → 0.5` at batch level doesn't ensure per-sample coherence. Regularization must target the right granularity. |\n| Character-level tokenizer | `tokenizer.py` + `autoencoder.py` | 74-char vocabulary for semantic embeddings produces poor representations. Always use subword tokenization (or better: pre-trained embeddings). |\n| Zombie test processes | System observation | Pytest processes with async tests can hang indefinitely if event loops aren't properly torn down. Always set test timeouts (`pytest-timeout`). |\n\n**Patterns worth preserving (good ideas in dead code):**\n\n| Pattern | Source File | Worth Keeping |\n|---------|------------|---------------|\n| Morphospace stability wells | `morphospace.py` | Concept of \"stability wells\" around HIHO=0.5 in latent space is sound theory. Re-implement in Phase 2 with real learned latent space. |\n| Trajectory prediction with physics | `predictor.py` | Momentum-based prediction and counterfactual branching (`imagine_branches()`) are good Phase 2 features. Preserve the API design. |\n| Multi-tier data collection | `experience_collector.py` | Parquet → SurrealDB → Vault → synthetic fallback hierarchy is good architecture. Reuse in new data pipeline. |\n| ExperienceEncoder layout | `experience_encoder.py` | The `[12D trajectory | 12 metrics | 5 op_type | ...]` prefix structure is well-designed. Keep for Phase 2, replace only the 227D fingerprint segment. |\n| Checkpoint format with config | `training.py` | Saving `{model, optimizer, config, epoch, metrics}` together is correct practice. Carry forward to new training pipeline. |\n| LCSP concept | `lcsp.py` | Latent Coherence Stability Predictor concept is valid for Phase 2 — predicting whether a latent point is in a stable region. |\n| Bioelectric field modeling | `bioelectric.py` | Mapping latent gradients to \"field strength\" for action generation is creative. Document for Phase 2 investigation. |\n\n**Vault storage plan:**\n1. Log each anti-pattern as a `vault_log_decision` (decision: \"don't do X because Y\")\n2. Extract each preservable pattern as a `vault_extract_pattern` (with code examples from the dead modules)\n3. Log the overall cleanup as a `vault_log_experiment` (hypothesis: \"dead code removal improves system health\")\n\n### Sprint 0 Task List\n\n- [ ] Task 0a: Kill zombie pytest processes (recover 7 GB RAM)\n- [ ] Task 0b: Install `nomic-embed-text` via Ollama\n- [ ] Task 0c: **EXTRACT**: Log 8 anti-patterns to vault via `vault_log_decision` (from existing broken FLUME code analysis)\n- [ ] Task 0d: **EXTRACT**: Log 7 preservable patterns to vault via `vault_extract_pattern` (stability wells, multi-tier collection, etc.)\n- [ ] Task 0e: Log overnight simulation stats to vault as experiment (5.5M trajectories, convergence data)\n\n---\n\n## Red Flag → Green Light Summary\n\n| # | Red Flag | Root Cause | Green Light Criteria | Phase |\n|---|----------|-----------|---------------------|-------|\n| 1 | KL collapse risk | No KL annealing, trains on noise | KL > 0.1, 128+ active latent units (of 256) | 1 |\n| 2 | Reconstruction fidelity unknown | Two VAEs, neither validated | cos_sim(input, reconstructed) > 0.95 on validation set | 1 |\n| 3 | Synthetic quality unknown | No generation/evaluation pipeline | Paraphrase Precision@1 > 0.85, Spearman ρ > 0.90 | 1 |\n| 4 | Evaluation metrics unvalidated | JourneyTracker uses SHA-256 hashes | Metrics correlate with task performance R² > 0.5 | 1+2 |\n| 5 | Integration impact unknown | FLUME only used for cache similarity | A/B: FLUME cache outperforms hash on precision-recall | 1+2 |\n| 6 | Universes team fit unclear | No mapping to their research needs | Clear alignment document with 3+ intersection points | 1 |\n\n---\n\n## Architecture: Fresh FLUME VAE\n\n### Core Insight\n\nDon't learn semantic embeddings from scratch with a tiny model. Use a **pre-trained embedding model** (Ollama `nomic-embed-text`, 768D) as input, then train a **proper VAE** to learn a structured, compressed 256D latent space on top of those embeddings.\n\n### Phase 1 Architecture: Semantic Cache Engine\n\n```\nText Input\n    │\n    ▼\n[Ollama nomic-embed-text]  ─── 768D dense semantic embedding\n    │\n    ▼\n[FLUME VAE Encoder]         ─── MLP: 768→512→512→384 → mu(256), logvar(256)\n    │\n    ▼\n256D latent z (mu used as embedding at inference)\n    │\n    ▼\n[FLUME VAE Decoder]         ─── MLP: 256→384→512→512→768 (reconstruct input)\n    │\n    ▼\n[SemanticCache]             ─── cosine similarity matching (threshold ~0.88)\n```\n\n**Why this works:**\n- Pre-trained embeddings already capture \"deploy API\" ≈ \"API deployment\"\n- VAE learns *structured* 256D space where distances are meaningful\n- KL regularization ensures smooth latent space (no holes between clusters)\n- Decoder forces VAE to retain information (reconstruction validates quality)\n- ~2.1M parameters — trains on CPU in minutes\n\n### Loss Function\n\n```\nL = L_recon + β·L_KL + λ_align·L_contrastive + λ_coh·L_coherence\n\nL_recon       = MSE(decoded, original_embedding)\nL_KL          = -0.5·Σ(1 + logvar - μ² - exp(logvar))    with free-bits per dimension\nL_contrastive = InfoNCE over known paraphrase pairs        (τ=0.07)\nL_coherence   = (mean(μ) - 0.5)²                          HIHO target\n```\n\n**KL collapse prevention (Red Flag 1):**\n- β annealing: 0.0 → 0.1 over first 30% of training steps\n- Free-bits: min 0.125 bits/dimension — forces each dimension to encode information\n- Active unit monitoring: alert if <128 of 256 dimensions are active\n\n### Phase 2 Architecture: Reasoning Latent Space\n\nExtends Phase 1 with a **temporal encoder** over trajectory sequences:\n\n```\nTrajectory Steps [(12D_state, metrics, op_type), ...]\n    │\n    ▼\n[Step Embedding]       ─── Linear(29→128) + positional encoding\n    │\n    ▼\n[Transformer Encoder]  ─── 2 layers, 4 heads, d_model=128\n    │\n    ▼\n[Attention Pooling → mu(256), logvar(256)]\n    │\n    ▼\n[Transformer Decoder]  ─── autoregressive trajectory reconstruction\n```\n\nEnables: interpolation between strategies, novel trajectory sampling, latent space navigation for agent guidance. JourneyTracker transitions from SHA-256 hashing to learned FLUME projections.\n\n---\n\n## Training Data Strategy (Hybrid)\n\n### Bootstrap (Phase 1 — immediate)\n\n1. **Structured synthetic tasks** (~5,000 samples)\n   - Task descriptions across 5 operation types (generate, analyze, search, transform, persist)\n   - Template groups with known paraphrases: `[\"deploy API\", \"API deployment\", \"launch API service\"]`\n   - Each embedded via `nomic-embed-text` → 768D vectors\n\n2. **Codebase docstrings** (~2,000 samples)\n   - Extract from Cohezion source via AST parsing\n   - Real domain-specific language\n\n3. **Vault experiment descriptions** (variable, ~500+ samples)\n   - From `~/vaults/cohezion-vault/experiments/*.json`\n\n4. **Paraphrase generation** (~15,000 pairs)\n   - Use Ollama to generate 3 paraphrases per task template\n   - Provides contrastive training pairs\n\n### Fine-tune (accumulates over time)\n\n5. **Real execution data pipeline**\n   - After each compound execution: store `(task_description, operation_type, metrics, skill_name)` as JSONL\n   - Path: `data/flume/experiences/execution_log.jsonl`\n   - Periodic re-embed + re-train (weekly or on-demand)\n\n### External (Phase 2)\n\n6. **Published reasoning datasets** (GSM8K chains, MATH solutions, code generation traces)\n   - For trajectory-level training in Phase 2\n\n---\n\n## Universes Team Alignment (Red Flag 6)\n\n**Source:** [Research Engineer, Universes](https://job-boards.greenhouse.io/anthropic/jobs/5061517008)\n\n### Mapping: Team Needs → FLUME Capabilities\n\n| Universes Team Need | FLUME Capability | Alignment |\n|---------------------|------------------|-----------|\n| **Agentic training environments** for extended-duration tasks | Compound engineering loop: 11-step execution pipeline with skill refinement, retrospection, and journey tracking. Literally an RL training environment for agentic tasks. | **Strong** |\n| **Evaluation frameworks measuring genuine capability gains** | Red Flag 4 resolution produces a validated evaluation framework with correlation-tested metrics. Phase 1 evaluation suite (reconstruction fidelity, similarity preservation, KL health, cache ablation) demonstrates evaluation engineering rigor. | **Strong** |\n| **Navigate ambiguity, manage interruptions, sustain context** | Phase 2 reasoning latent space compresses reasoning trajectories for context sustainability. 256D embeddings preserve semantic meaning across long interactions. Morphospace navigation handles ambiguity via stability wells. | **Strong** (Phase 2) |\n| **RL environments and simulation platforms** | 12D universe simulation with FLUME VAE as the representation layer. JourneyTracker records state transitions. DegradationDetector monitors drift. This is RL infrastructure. | **Strong** |\n| **Integrate into production training systems** | Phase 1 semantic cache engine is a production system. Demonstrates ability to ship ML infrastructure, not just research prototypes. | **Strong** |\n| **Rapidly troubleshoot across research and production ML** | This spec itself — diagnosing architectural fractures, designing fixes, implementing with TDD — demonstrates this capability directly. | **Meta** |\n\n### Preferred Qualifications Match\n\n| Qualification | Evidence |\n|---------------|----------|\n| LLM training/fine-tuning/evaluation | VAE training pipeline, evaluation framework, metric validation |\n| RL environments, simulation platforms | 12D universe simulation, compound loop as RL env |\n| Large-scale ML infrastructure | SemanticCache, ExperienceCollector, multi-tier data pipeline |\n| Containerization, distributed systems | Cloud Run deployment, Tailscale Funnel for MCP, multi-session worktree pattern |\n\n### Positioning Document (Deliverable)\n\nProduce a concise document (max 2 pages) that:\n1. Frames FLUME as an **agentic training environment** with validated evaluation\n2. Highlights the **evaluation engineering** (not just the model)\n3. Shows **production readiness** (Phase 1 cache engine in production)\n4. Positions the **compound engineering loop** as an RL training methodology\n5. Demonstrates research taste: honest red-flag identification, systematic resolution\n\n---\n\n## Implementation Plan\n\n### Phase 1 Scope (This Spec)\n\n**New files:**\n\n| File | Purpose | ~Lines |\n|------|---------|--------|\n| `src/cohezion/flume/vae.py` | Core VAE model (encoder, decoder, reparameterization, loss) | 200 |\n| `src/cohezion/flume/embedding_provider.py` | Pre-trained embedding interface (Ollama + hash fallback) | 120 |\n| `src/cohezion/flume/train_vae.py` | Training pipeline (KL annealing, free-bits, contrastive) | 250 |\n| `src/cohezion/flume/evaluate_vae.py` | Evaluation framework (all red flag metrics) | 200 |\n| `src/cohezion/flume/data_pipeline.py` | Data prep (synthetic tasks, embedding, contrastive pairs) | 180 |\n| `scripts/train_flume_v2.py` | CLI training entry point | 50 |\n| `tests/flume/test_vae_v2.py` | VAE model unit tests | 150 |\n| `tests/flume/test_embedding_provider.py` | Embedding provider tests | 100 |\n| `tests/flume/test_train_vae_v2.py` | Training pipeline tests | 150 |\n| `tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py` | Evaluation framework tests | 120 |\n| `tests/flume/test_data_pipeline.py` | Data pipeline tests | 100 |\n\n**Modified files:**\n\n| File | Change | Risk |\n|------|--------|------|\n| `src/cohezion/flume/vae_encoder.py` | Load new VAE model, use EmbeddingProvider. Public interface unchanged. | Low |\n| `src/cohezion/flume/__init__.py` | Add new exports | Low |\n| `src/cohezion/compound/journey_tracker.py` | Optional FLUME encoder path in `text_to_latent()` | Low |\n| `src/cohezion/swarm/semantic_cache.py` | Threshold validation after new model (may need tuning) | Low |\n\n**Legacy files** (not removed, but superseded by new implementations — knowledge extracted to vault in Sprint 0):\n`autoencoder.py`, `training.py`, `dataset.py` and 12 disconnected modules remain on disk but are not imported by new code.\n\n### Task Breakdown\n\n**Sprint 1: Foundation + Positioning** *(context: query vault first, then build)*\n\n- [ ] Task 1: Write Universes team positioning document (guides all subsequent sprint decisions)\n- [ ] Task 2: `embedding_provider.py` — TDD:\n  - RED: Compound execution → data logged → text embedded → cache lookup succeeds\n  - GREEN: Full pipeline verified\n  - Log as vault experiment with compound metrics\n\n### Key Integration Points\n\n**`vae_encoder.py`** (primary integration surface):\n```\nCurrent: text → SHA-256 hash → expand to 256D → MLP encoder → mu → 256D\nNew:     text → Ollama embed → 768D → VAE encoder → mu → 256D\n         (fallback: text → SHA-256 hash → expand to 256D if Ollama unavailable)\n```\n\n**`semantic_cache.py`** (consumer — no interface changes):\n```\nFlumeVAEEmbeddingModel.encode(text) → EmbeddingResult(embedding=[...256 floats...])\n# Same interface, better embeddings\n```\n\n**`journey_tracker.py`** (Phase 1 minimal change):\n```\ntext_to_latent(text):\n    if flume_encoder available:\n        flume_256d = flume_encoder.encode(text)\n        return np.tile(flume_256d, 8)  # 256×8 = 2048D for existing pipeline\n    else:\n        return hash_based_latent(text)  # existing behavior\n```\n\n---\n\n## Verification Strategy\n\n### Per Red Flag\n\n| Red Flag | Verification Command/Method | Pass Criteria |\n|----------|----------------------------|---------------|\n| 1. KL collapse | `uv run python -m scripts.train_flume_v2 --evaluate-kl` | Active units ≥ 128/256, KL > 0.1 |\n| 2. Reconstruction | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k reconstruction` | cos_sim(input, recon) > 0.95 on validation |\n| 3. Synthetic quality | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k paraphrase` | Precision@1 > 0.85 |\n| 4. Metrics validation | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k similarity_preservation` | Spearman ρ > 0.90 between original and latent similarities |\n| 5. Integration impact | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k cache_ablation` | FLUME precision-recall dominates hash baseline |\n| 6. Team fit | Read positioning document, verify 3+ alignment points | Qualitative: convincing narrative |\n\n### Full Suite\n\n```bash\n# Unit tests\nuv run pytest tests/flume/ -v\n\n# Train and evaluate\nuv run python scripts/train_flume_v2.py --config default --evaluate\n\n# Integration test\nuv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -v\n\n# Existing test suite (no regressions)\nuv run pytest tests/ -q\n```\n\n---\n\n## Adversarial Review Findings\n\nThree-perspective review per vault protocol (ADR 2026-02-14). All findings addressed in the plan above.\n\n### Feasibility Challenger\n\n| Finding | Severity | Resolution |\n|---------|----------|------------|\n| `nomic-embed-text` not installed in Ollama | HIGH | Added Task 0b: `ollama pull nomic-embed-text` |\n| 768D→256D is only 3x compression — bottleneck may not force meaningful structure learning | MEDIUM | Contrastive loss (InfoNCE) provides additional learning signal beyond reconstruction. If bottleneck proves insufficient, reduce decoder capacity or add dropout. Monitor active units. |\n| ~22,500 training samples for 2.1M params = overfitting risk | MEDIUM | Weight decay (1e-4), dropout (0.1), early stopping (patience=10), and data augmentation (paraphrases). Phase 1 is a starting point; real data accumulates. |\n| Ollama network dependency at inference | MEDIUM | Fallback chain in EmbeddingProvider: Ollama → cached embeddings → SHA-256 hash. Cache hot embeddings in LRU cache (text→768D). |\n| Paraphrase generation quality depends on Ollama model | LOW | Use simple templates + programmatic augmentation (word reordering, synonym substitution) before LLM generation. LLM paraphrases are supplementary, not primary. |\n\n### Strategic Reviewer\n\n| Finding | Severity | Resolution |\n|---------|----------|------------|\n| Phase 1 (cache engine) doesn't demonstrate RL capability — weak positioning | HIGH | Move positioning document to Sprint 1 (not Sprint 4). Frame Phase 1 as \"evaluation engineering rigor\" and Phase 2 as \"RL environment.\" The investigation and systematic red-flag resolution ARE the demonstration of research engineering quality. |\n| Overnight simulation data (5.5M trajectories) = irreplaceable Phase 2 training data | CRITICAL | Changed from \"delete\" to \"compress and preserve.\" Added data characterization task. |\n| Green-light criteria are internally defined — may not match what Universes team values | MEDIUM | Positioning document (moved to Sprint 1) explicitly maps our metrics to team needs. Adjust criteria if mapping reveals misalignment. |\n| Experience-guided execution (lesson-37) validates hybrid data approach | LOW | Confirmed — our plan aligns with proven vault lesson. |\n\n### Risk & Completeness Reviewer\n\n| Finding | Severity | Resolution |\n|---------|----------|------------|\n| experience_collector/encoder/pipeline/dataset must be preserved | HIGH | No files deleted — all existing code stays on disk. New code supersedes, doesn't replace. |\n| Vault's 6-step process (ADR 2026-02-09) — EXTRACT step is critical | HIGH | Sprint 0 extracts anti-patterns and patterns to vault before building new code |\n| Overnight simulation data is irreplaceable Phase 2 training data | CRITICAL | Preserved.    126→    def pack(self) -> str:
   127→        """Pack 12D state into a compact base64 string for storage."""
   128→        arr = self.to_array()
   129→        if hasattr(arr, "tobytes"):
   130→            try:
   131→                return base64.b64encode(arr.tobytes()).decode("ascii")
   132→            except Exception as e:
   133→                logger.debug("Binary pack failed, using JSON fallback: %s", e)
   134→        # Fallback to JSON string as 'packed' if numpy is missing
   135→        return base64.b64encode(str(arr).encode()).decode("ascii")
   136→
   137→    @classmethod
   138→    def unpack(cls, packed: str) -> "PhysicsState":
   139→        """Unpack 12D state from base64 string."""
   140→        data = base64.b64decode(packed)
   141→        # Ensure numpy is available as np
   142→        import numpy as np
   143→
   144→        arr = np.frombuffer(data, dtype=np.float32)
   145→        return cls.from_array(arr)

   162→        content_val = self.content

   165→        if compress and len(self.content) > 100:

   172→            "content": content_val,
   173→            "embedding": self.embedding,

   175→            "node_type": self.node_type,

   177→            "metadata": self.metadata,
   178→            "compressed": is_compressed or self.compressed,

   219→    async def is_alive(self) -> bool:
   220→        """Check if the SurrealDB server is responsive."""
   221→        try:
   222→            # Try to hit the health endpoint
   223→            # We use the URL to derive the health port (usually same as RPC but HTTP)
   224→            health_url = self.url.replace("ws://", "http://").replace("/rpc", "/health")
   225→            async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=1.0) as client:
   226→                response = await client.get(health_url)
   227→                return response.status_code == 200
   228→        except Exception:
   229→            return False
   230→
   231→    async def ensure_active(self, timeout: int = 30) -> bool:
   232→        """
   233→        Ensures the database is available. Blocks until active.
   234→        If it's down, logs a critical error.
   235→        """
   236→        if SurrealClient._FAILED_ONCE:
   237→            return False
   238→
   239→        start = time.time()
   240→        while time.time() - start < timeout:
   241→            if await self.is_alive():
   242→                logger.info("📡 SurrealDB existence confirmed.")
   243→                return True
   244→            logger.warning("⏳ Waiting for SurrealDB substrate...")
   245→            await asyncio.sleep(2)
   246→
   247→        logger.error("❌ SurrealDB substrate FAILURE: Persistence guard timeout.")
   248→        SurrealClient._FAILED_ONCE = True  # One failure is enough
   249→        return False

   251→    def __init__(
   252→        self,

   266→    async def connect(self) -> bool:
   267→        """

   270→        Returns True if connected successfully.

   272→        breaker = get_circuit("surrealdb", failure_threshold=5)
   273→        if not breaker.allow_request():
   274→            logger.warning("🛑 Circuit Open: SurrealDB connection rejected. Using fallback.")
   275→            self._use_fallback()
   276→            return True
   277→

   302→            await self._client.use(self.namespace, self.database)
   303→            self._connected = True

   305→            breaker.record_success()

Here's what I found regarding SurrealDB 3.0, its release, and the Python SDK:

## SurrealDB 3.0 Release

SurrealDB v3.0.0 was released on February 17, 2026. SurrealDB 3.0 represents a significant step forward in the stability and maturity of the database. By separating values from expressions, introducing computed fields, adopting ID‑based storage, enabling synced writes by default, and redesigning document handling, SurrealDB becomes more predictable, more durable, and easier to operate.

### Key Features in 3.0
- **Surrealism** — a new open-source extension system for SurrealDB that allows you to define modular, programmable logic using functions written in Rust and execute them directly within the database at runtime. Support for other languages including JavaScript and Python is planned.
- **Surreal Sync** — a new tool that helps move data from another database or source into SurrealDB, currently in active development.
- **Client-side transactions** — letting developers keep application logic in SDK languages such as Rust, Go, or JavaScript, while SurrealDB manages transactional consistency. Complex, multi-step business logic can now be expressed naturally in application code without giving up transactional safety.
- Other highlights include GraphQL support now being stable, custom API endpoints, computed fields, record references, and bug fixes & SDK improvements.

### Migration
To migrate from a 2.x version to 3.0, SurrealDB provides a page with further detail on the breaking changes and how to use migration diagnostics to obtain a list of actions for your particular schema.

## Python SDK Compatibility

The Python SDK requires Python version 3.10 or greater and is available as a PyPI package. The SDK works seamlessly with SurrealDB versions v2.0.0 to v3.0.0, ensuring compatibility with the latest version.

The GitHub README for the Python SDK appears slightly behind, as it still notes compatibility with "SurrealDB versions v2.0.0 to v2.3.6." However, the official SurrealDB docs confirm v3.0.0 support.

### SDK Ecosystem
Alongside the 3.0 release, the Go and Java SDKs have reached 1.0 production readiness, giving developers stable integration paths in more ecosystems.

### Installation
You can install the Python SDK via pip (`pip install surrealdb`) or uv (`uv add surrealdb`).

# SurrealDB Python SDK: Version Support & Key APIs

## SurrealDB 3.0 Compatibility

The README does not mention SurrealDB 3.0 at all. The documentation focuses exclusively on **v2.x versions**, specifically v2.0.5, v2.1.8, v2.2.6, and v2.3.6. There is no mention of beta or pre-release support for version 3.0.

## Tested Versions

According to the documentation, the SDK maintains compatibility across multiple v2.x releases:
- "Tests are designed to be version-agnostic across all supported SurrealDB v2.x versions"

The CI/CD testing matrix covers "key stable versions (v2.0.5, v2.1.8, v2.2.6, v2.3.6)" on every pull request.

## Key API Methods

The SDK provides core database operations through both async and blocking interfaces:

**Primary CRUD Operations:**
- `create()` – Insert new records
- `select()` – Read/query records
- `update()` – Modify existing records
- `delete()` – Remove records
- `query()` – Execute SurrealQL statements

**Connection & Authentication:**
- `signin()` – Authenticate users
- `use()` – Select namespace and database

**Advanced Features (WebSocket only):**
- `new_session()` – Create scoped sessions
- `begin_transaction()` – Start transactional operations
- `commit()` / `cancel()` – Transaction management
```

# SurrealDB 3.0: Key Changes and Migration Guidance

## Breaking Changes

The blog post doesn't explicitly list breaking changes, but several architectural shifts will require updates:

1. **Futures → Computed Fields**: The `future` type is replaced with computed fields using the `COMPUTED` keyword. Old syntax like `VALUE <future>` no longer works.

2. **Document Structure**: Records now use a proper document wrapper separating content from metadata, which may affect how results are rendered.

3. **ID-Based Storage**: Catalog entries (namespaces, databases, indexes) now use ID-based rather than name-based storage internally.

## Python SDK Updates

The post mentions that "the Go and Java SDKs have reached `1.0` production readiness," but provides no specific Python SDK migration details. Users should consult the official migration guide.

## Migration Resources

The article references: "Update your SurrealDB instance to 3.0 with our [migration guide](https://surrealdb.com/docs/surrealdb/installation/upgrading/migrating-data-to-3x)" but doesn't detail its contents.

## AI Agent Memory Features

Three capabilities specifically support AI agent applications:

- **File Support**: Store images, audio, and documents alongside structured records
- **Enhanced Vector Search**: Millisecond-precision embedding retrieval and indexing
- **Surrealism Extensions**: Execute custom WebAssembly functions for "AI integration, model execution" directly within the database
```

# SurrealDB 2.x to 3.x Migration: Breaking Changes & Steps

## Critical Breaking Changes (Will Break)

1. **Futures → COMPUTED Fields**
   - Replace `VALUE <future> { expression }` with `COMPUTED expression`
   - Futures stored in records have no direct replacement and require redesign
   - COMPUTED fields cannot be nested or combined with VALUE, DEFAULT, READONLY, ASSERT, REFERENCE, or FLEXIBLE

2. **Function Name Updates**
   - `duration::from::*` → `duration::from_*` (e.g., `duration::from_days`)
   - `string::is::*` → `string::is_*` (e.g., `string::is_email`)
   - `time::is::*` → `time::is_*` (e.g., `time::is_leap_year`)
   - `type::is::*` → `type::is_*` (e.g., `type::is_array`)
   - `rand::guid()` → `rand::id()`
   - `type::thing` → `type::record`

3. **array::range Syntax Change**
   - Old: `array::range(offset, count)` 
   - New: `array::range(start, end)` or use ranges like `array::range(0..=5)`

4. **LET Required for Parameters**
   - Change `$val = 10;` to `LET $val = 10;`

5. **GROUP and SPLIT Conflict**
   - Cannot use both in same query; wrap one clause in a subquery

6. **Removed Like Operators**
   - `~`, `!~`, `?~`, `*~` removed; use `string::similarity::jaro()` or `string::distance::osa()` instead

7. **Index Analyzer Updates**
   - `SEARCH ANALYZER` → `FULLTEXT ANALYZER`

8. **Vector Index Changes**
   - `MTREE` → `HNSW` for vector search indexes

9. **Strictness Configuration**
   - Move from instance-level `--strict` flag to `DEFINE DATABASE mydb STRICT;`

10. **Stored Closures Removed**
    - Cannot store closures in records; remove entirely

## Likely Breaking Changes (Can Break)

- **`.*` idiom behavior** differs for arrays/objects
- **Optional chaining syntax**: `?` becomes `.?`
- **Set type** now deduplicates and orders items, displays as `{}`
- **SCHEMAFULL tables** reject extra fields (previously silently filtered)
- **Non-existent tables** now error instead of returning empty arrays

## Automation Available

Use Surrealist's migration diagnostics tool (v2.6.1+) to automatically identify issues and export V3-compatible data with automatic transformations applied.
```

   412→        breaker = get_circuit("surrealdb")
   413→        try:

   423→            breaker.record_failure()

   425→            raise

   427→    async def _query_inmemory(self, sql: str, vars: dict[str, Any] | None) -> Any:
   428→        """InMemoryStore query handler with basic SQL-like operations."""
   429→        if "CREATE missions" in sql or "CREATE agent_journey" in sql:
   430→            data = vars.get("data") if vars else None
   431→            if data:
   432→                bare_id = data["id"].split(":")[-1] if ":" in data["id"] else data["id"]
   433→                self._client.store(bare_id, data)
   434→            return [data] if data else []
   435→        if "CREATE agent_thought" in sql:
   436→            data = vars.get("data") if vars else None
   437→            if data:
   438→                self._client.store(data["id"], data)
   439→            return [data] if data else []
   440→        if "CREATE velocity_events" in sql:
   441→            data = vars.get("data") if vars else None
   442→            if data:
   443→                self._client.store(f"event_{int(time.time() * 1000)}", data)
   444→            return [data] if data else []
   445→        if "FROM missions" in sql or "FROM agent_journey" in sql:
   446→            mission_id = vars.get("id") if vars else None
   447→            if not mission_id:
   448→                match = re.search(r"FROM\s+[\w`]+:([\w-]+)", sql)
   449→                if match:
   450→                    mission_id = match.group(1)
   451→
   452→            mission = self._client.get(mission_id) if mission_id else None
   453→            if not mission and not mission_id:
   454→                all_items = self._client.get_all(limit=100)
   455→                return [{"result": all_items, "status": "OK"}]
   456→
   457→            return [{"result": [mission] if mission else [], "status": "OK"}]
   458→        if "FROM agent_thought" in sql:
   459→            all_nodes = self._client.get_all(1000)
   460→            matches = [n for n in all_nodes if n.get("node_type") == "agent_thought"]
   461→
   462→            if "qh" in sql or "metadata.query_hash" in sql:
   463→                processed = []
   464→                for m in matches:
   465→                    meta = m.get("metadata", {})
   466→                    processed.append(
   467→                        {
   468→                            "content": m.get("content"),
   469→                            "qh": meta.get("query_hash"),
   470→                            "agent": meta.get("agent"),
   471→                        }
   472→                    )
   473→                return [processed]
   474→
   475→            return [matches]
   476→
   477→        if "SELECT * FROM" in sql and "WHERE" in sql:
   478→            if "node_type = 'energy_snapshot'" in sql:
   479→                all_items = self._client.get_all(1000)
   480→                results = []
   481→                for item in all_items:
   482→                    if item.get("node_type") != "energy_snapshot":
   483→                        continue
   484→
   485→                    ps = item.get("physics_state", {})
   486→
   487→                    if "coherence > 0.9" in sql and ps.get("dim_12_coherence", 0) <= 0.9:
   488→                        continue
   489→                    if "stability > 0.9" in sql and ps.get("dim_10_stability", 0) <= 0.9:
   490→                        continue
   491→
   492→                    results.append(item)
   493→
   494→                return [{"result": results, "status": "OK"}]
   495→
   496→            match_from = re.search(r"FROM\s+(\w+)", sql)
   497→            if match_from:
   498→                match_where = re.search(r"WHERE\s+(\w+)\s*=\s*\$(\w+)", sql)
   499→                if match_where and vars:
   500→                    field = match_where.group(1)
   501→                    var_name = match_where.group(2)
   502→
   503→                    if var_name in vars:
   504→                        target_value = vars[var_name]
   505→                        all_items = self._client.get_all(1000)
   506→
   507→                        filtered_items = [
   508→                            item for item in all_items if item.get(field) == target_value
   509→                        ]
   510→                        return [filtered_items]
   511→
   512→        logger.warning("Query not supported in InMemoryStore")
   513→        return []
   514→
   515→    async def get_node(self, node_id: str) -> UniverseNode | None:
   516→        """Retrieve a node by ID."""
   517→        if not self._connected:
   518→            await self.connect()
   519→
   520→        try:
   521→            if isinstance(self._client, InMemoryStore):
   522→                data = self._client.get(node_id)
   523→            else:
   524→                result = await self._client.select(f"universe_nodes:{node_id}")
   525→                data = result[0] if result else None
   526→
   527→            if not data:
   528→                return None
   529→
   530→            return self._dict_to_node(data)
   531→
   532→        except Exception as e:
   533→            logger.error(f"Failed to get node: {e}")
   534→            return None
   535→
